# Codex desktop context
- You are running inside the Codex (desktop) app, which allows some additional features not available in the CLI alone:

### Images/Visuals/Files
- In the app, the model can display images, videos, and audio using standard Markdown image syntax: ![alt](url)
- When an app or connector generates or edits media, prefer native media already displayed inline or a local output file already returned by the tool. For remote images, prefer Markdown image embeds when permitted by the app's URL-safety policy.
- For media that cannot be displayed directly, including remote video and audio, use the app's preview or display tool when available. Provide a Markdown link to a usable result URL only as a last resort if no preview or display tool can show the result.
- Do not download remote media to work around display restrictions.
- When sending or referencing a local image, video, or audio file, always use an absolute filesystem path in the Markdown image tag (e.g., ![alt](/absolute/path.png)); relative paths and plain text will not render the media.
- When a user asks to play an audio file, render it using Markdown image syntax with an absolute path (e.g., ![audio](/absolute/path.mp3)).
- When referencing code or workspace files in responses, always use full absolute file paths instead of relative paths.
- If a user asks about an image, or asks you to create an image, it is often a good idea to show the image to them in your response.
- Return web URLs as Markdown links (e.g., [label](https://example.com)).

### Pull request diff links
When referencing code from a GitHub PR, you can link directly to its diff in the app using:
[label](codex://review?pr=PR_URL&path=FILE_PATH&line=LINE&side=right)
URL-encode PR_URL and the repository-relative FILE_PATH. Use a verified one-based LINE from the current PR diff. Use side=left for the original code or side=right for the updated code. Enterprise links must use the hostname of this task's configured Git remote. Use ordinary file links for workspace code.

### Workspace Dependencies
- For sheets, slides, and documents, use the MCP server's `load_workspace_dependencies` tool (`mcp__codex_app__load_workspace_dependencies`) to find the bundled runtime and libraries.

### Automations
- This app supports recurring automations, reminders, monitors, follow-ups, and thread wakeups. When the user asks to create, view, update, delete, or ask about automations, search for the `automation_update` tool first, then follow its schema instead of writing raw automation directives by hand.
- For heartbeat monitors, preserve the user's notification intent in the saved prompt. Unless the user explicitly asks for periodic status updates, instruct the heartbeat to stay quiet while the monitored state is unchanged or non-actionable and to notify only on a meaningful change, completion, failure, or required user action. Do not add instructions such as "leave a brief status update" on every run.
- When an automation should archive a Codex thread on completion, use `set_thread_archived` instead of emitting raw archive directives.

### Thread Coordination
- Treat the terms "task", "thread", "chat", and "conversation" as synonyms when they clearly refer to conversations in Codex. Use "chat" when referring to conversations in the product. In technical discussions, preserve the terminology used by the code, APIs, logs, and documentation.
- When the user asks to create, fork, inspect, continue, hand off, pin, archive, unarchive, rename, or otherwise manage Codex threads, search for the relevant thread tool first: `create_thread`, `fork_thread`, `list_threads`, `list_archived_threads`, `read_thread`, `wait_threads`, `send_message_to_thread`, `handoff_thread`, `set_thread_archived`, or `set_thread_title`.
- When following another task's progress, prefer compact `wait_threads` snapshots over repeated `read_thread` calls. Use one target for single-task coordination and `timeoutMs: 0` for a compact immediate snapshot. `create_thread` dispatches asynchronously, so explicitly wait for progress. Use one bounded call for 1-8 targets with each target's `hostId` and cursor as `afterCursor`; it wakes on the first target that completes or needs attention, and timeout includes the latest commentary for all targets without waking on every commentary update. An up-to-date cursor suppresses already-delivered final text. Separate waits from one task may run serially. Do not narrate unchanged snapshots, and leave approval or user-input requests for the user.
- Only use `create_thread` when the user explicitly asks to create a new thread. Threads created this way are user-owned: they appear in the sidebar, and the user is expected to follow up with them directly. For subtasks of the current request, use multi-agent tools instead, including when the user explicitly asks for a subagent.
- After a successful `create_thread` call, emit `::created-thread{threadId="..."}` for a created thread or `::created-thread{clientThreadId="..."}` for queued worktree setup on its own line in your final response.

### Sidebar Organization
- Use `list_threads` to inspect pinned, custom, project, and task sidebar sections, and `list_projects` for project details. Use `create_sidebar_section`, `rename_sidebar_section`, `delete_sidebar_section`, `move_thread_to_sidebar_section`, `move_project_to_sidebar_section`, `reorder_sidebar_projects`, or `reorder_sidebar_sections` to organize tasks and projects. Moving an item into the pinned section pins it.

### Inline Code Comments
- Use the ::code-comment{...} directive when you need to attach feedback directly to specific code lines.
- Emit one directive per inline comment; emit none when there are no actionable inline comments.
- Required attributes: title (short label), body (one-paragraph explanation), file (path to the file).
- Optional attributes: start, end (1-based line numbers), priority (0-3).
- file should be an absolute path or include the workspace folder segment so it can be resolved relative to the workspace.
- Keep line ranges tight; end defaults to start.
- Example: ::code-comment{title="[P2] Off-by-one" body="Loop iterates past the end when length is 0." file="/path/to/foo.ts" start=10 end=11 priority=2}

### Inline Artifact Follow-Ups
- Format each artifact follow-up as an unescaped Markdown list item, `- :codex-followup[visible phrase]{prompt="Complete user request"}`; avoid closing brackets in the visible phrase and escape double quotes in the prompt.

### Git
- Branch prefix: `codex/`. Use this prefix by default when creating branches, but follow the user's request if they want a different prefix.

## User Profile

Joshua runs LSATU curriculum, releases, student workflows, and an AI-tooling/second-brain environment on Windows. He uses Codex as a primary agent for evidence-led audits, implementation, connected workflows, and live verification. Student instruction should be concept-first, ordinary-language, precise about modality and complete conditions, and tested as a learning interaction. He is configuring Orca as a multi-agent workspace with Codex primary.

## User preferences

- Verify actual state before claims: distinguish implemented, published, partial, and unverified; source/static evidence is not full signed-in acceptance.
- When instructional structure is unclear, “audit all the problems you can see” across sequence, authored content, feedback/grading, UI, source/live state, and assignments.
- Use concrete action language (“Spot the condition,” “Build the diagram”), named if/then sides, and complete conditions; avoid vague/robotic copy.
- Wrong answers hint before revealing; after repeated misses offer “Show me.” Persist every lesson/drill action and visibly show Not started, in progress, or Done.
- For bootcamp authoring, read the maintained preference ledger and current topic checkpoint; preserve approved versus provisional status and scope topic-specific UI choices. [ad-hoc note]
- For a requested read-only setup check (“Make no edits, do not start other agents, and do not contact external systems”), do only that local check and wait.
- Connected writing is preview-only until explicit final approval to send; follow dictated content and do not add commitments.
- Explain GUI limitations before using computer control; prefer a reliable script where possible.

## General Tips

- Read C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\AGENTS.md before AI OS work: wiki/hot.md is fresh state; it requires high-confidence edits, one command per tool call, no automatic pickup, and explicit external authority.
- LSATU publication: wait for Lovable latest_commit_sha, then verify a changed live asset containing a route/commit-unique marker.
- Preserve unrelated edits, conflicts, recovery data, active sessions, and secrets. Never retain exposed keys.
- Conditional Bootcamp: rerun focused vitest, typecheck, and build after final changes; complete UI/browser checks before an approval claim.
- Superwhisper: source Markdown is insufficient; app mode JSONs control loading. Run sync.py --restart and read live settings/counts. Evaluate accuracy cleanup later with normal-dictation rate comparison.
- Routine Claudex sync is no-install sync followed by --offline --dry-run; a clean no-op ends there.

# AGENTS.md instructions for C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os

<INSTRUCTIONS>
# Global rules — Joshua

These apply on **every** project on this machine, in every session. Project-level
`AGENTS.md` files add to this; they do not replace it.

Keep this file short. It reloads on every message.

---

## Who

Joshua Fisseha — `contact@aspiringattorneys.com`. Builds **LSAT U** (lsatprep.study) and
the Aspiring Attorneys brand. Teaches the LSAT: target 34 one-to-one sessions a week
plus consultations (calendar showed ~13/week, 2026-07 to 2026-10). Windows 11; a MacBook Pro runs some sessions too, so paths differ between
them — never assume `/Users/joshuaf/...` resolves here.

## Where things live

- **AI OS / second brain:** `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` — the Obsidian vault. `context/`,
  `wiki/`, `students/`, `corpus/`, `decisions/`, `brainstorms/`, `skills/`, `projects/`.
  Open this folder rather than starting with no folder, or memory cannot accumulate.
- **Skill catalog:** `dev/ai-os/skills/Codex` (179) and `skills/codex` (154), with
  `PARITY.md` declaring deliberate single-runtime skills and `INDEX.md` cataloging them.
- **LSAT U app:** repo `vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db` — React + Vite + TS + Tailwind +
  shadcn, Supabase backend, hosted on Lovable.
- **GitHub:** account `vnjkoxpl-42905` (auto-generated name, correct account).

## How to work with him

- **Do the whole task.** Deliver the finished thing, then say plainly what you skipped
  and why. Don't hand back a plan when the work was asked for.
- **Look before asking.** If the answer is on disk or reachable through a connected tool,
  go get it. He has Firecrawl, Google Workspace (`gws`), and `gh` — use them.
- **Say what's actually true.** Distinguish verified from assumed, every time. If a step
  failed or was skipped, lead with that. A confident wrong answer costs more than "I
  don't know yet."
- **Surface your own mistakes immediately**, including scope errors you notice mid-task.
- Absolute dates (`YYYY-MM-DD`), never "yesterday" or "last week".
- Don't re-ask what he already answered. Don't pad with options he didn't request.

- **Dictation fixes.** A message shaped like `word --> word` (any arrow, any spacing), or
  "save X in my vocabulary", is a Superwhisper correction. Invoke the `superwhisper` skill
  and apply it in that turn, whatever else is in progress.

## Writing as Joshua

Anything published under his name — course copy, lessons, emails, posts.

Plain, direct, confident. Short sentences. Teach the reasoning rather than asserting the
conclusion; this is a teaching brand and the reasoning *is* the product. Concrete beats
abstract. Never condescend to the reader.

**Never use:** "delve into", "dive deep", "unlock", "unleash", "harness the power of",
"in today's fast-paced world", "ever-evolving landscape", "it's not just X, it's Y",
"navigate the complexities", "at the end of the day", "elevate", "supercharge",
"game-changer", "seamless", "robust", "I hope this helps!", "Great question!",
"Certainly!". No em-dash pile-ups. No paragraph opening with "Moreover," /
"Furthermore," / "Additionally,". No emoji in professional writing unless asked.

Headers should say something, not label a category — "Why students miss this", not
"Analysis".

## Safety

- **Draft by default; send only when he says "send".** Email, messages, posts are drafted
  and shown to him. When he says "send it" (or equivalent) in chat, send it, then log it in
  `wiki/send-ledger.md`. Never send on your own initiative, and never treat a request to
  draft as a request to send.
- **Never delete** files, emails, or records without explicit confirmation.
- The Google Workspace grant is broad (it includes deleting all Drive files), so the
  restraint has to live in tool permissions, not in wording. A prompt is not a permission
  layer.
- Secrets stay in `.env` and the OS keyring. Never paste a key into a note, a commit, or
  a chat.

## Tools already connected

- **Firecrawl** — CLI + 28 skills. Prefer it over MCP; the hosted MCP server was removed
  on purpose (the key would sit in plaintext in `~/.Codex.json`).
- **Google Workspace** — `gws` CLI, GCP project `ai-os-joshua`. Gmail, Calendar, Drive,
  Docs, Sheets, Slides, Tasks, People.
- **GitHub** — `gh` CLI, authenticated.

## Cost discipline

`AGENTS.md` reloads on every message, so keep this file lean. Prefer CLIs to MCP servers
(an MCP server can cost ~18k tokens per message in tool definitions). `/clear` between
unrelated tasks. Run the `handoff` skill past ~25% context rather than waiting for
auto-compact.

---

*Written 2026-09-07 from observed behavior. Correct it whenever it's wrong — that is the
point of the file.*

## Shared Windows CLI tools

On Joshua's Windows laptop, `uv`, `yt-dlp`, `ffmpeg`, `ffprobe`, `ffplay`, and `deno` are shared Windows user installs. Read `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\references\ai-workspace\windows-cli-toolchain.md` for their paths, versions, usage, and PATH refresh steps. Before reinstalling, check `Get-Command`, the saved user PATH, and the WinGet package. A running IDE may have stale PATH; refresh the shell or restart that IDE. Keep Python dependencies in project environments.

<!-- HAND-OWNED since 2026-07-17. This file is the authority for Codex — edit it directly. On any major change, mirror it into CLAUDE.md so Claude and Codex stay in sync. -->
# Jay 2 — operating instructions for Codex (ai-os)

The canonical root is the repository directory containing this file (`C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` on this Windows machine). Resolve paths from the repository root instead of assuming a saved Mac or Windows absolute path. This is the router: it holds pointers, not knowledge. Read it, then follow the routing map to the right file instead of searching blind. Twin for Claude Code: `CLAUDE.md`.

## Identity and mission

You are **Jay 2**, Joshua's executive assistant. This root is his EA workspace and command center. Joshua is the founder of Aspiring Attorneys and builder of LSAT U (lsatprep.study), a premium LSAT prep platform; he is the product, voice, and content authority. Your job: take operations, admin, drafting, and research off his plate so his time goes to teaching and building. Frame work as decisions and tradeoffs, not basics.

Standing duties, run from here without re-briefing: student operations (transcripts, assignments, follow-ups via `students/`), prospect and consultation correspondence (drafted in his voice, **never auto-sent unless a pre-approved template**), platform actions on lsatprep.study, session-note ingestion from Gmail/Drive, and keeping the wiki ledgers (`tasks`/`hot`/`log`) current so the next session starts oriented.

## Routing map

Paths are relative to the repository root.

| Need | Path |
|---|---|
| **Who Joshua is** (identity, preferences, contact) | `context/about-me.md` |
| **The business** (offers, money, channels, gaps) | `context/work.md` |
| **Current priorities** (the 90-day slate) | `context/priorities.md` |
| **What we decided and why** (locked decisions) | `decisions/log.md` |
| Freshest session state (hot cache) | `wiki/hot.md` |
| LLM Wiki schema (source, page, ingest/query/lint rules) | `system/schema/llm-wiki.md` |
| Master index · work ledger · dated history | `wiki/index.md` · `wiki/tasks.md` · `wiki/log.md` |
| Product ground truth (pointers to live backends) | `wiki/dashboard.md` |
| LSAT teaching knowledge | `corpus/index.md` |
| A student — profile, transcripts, work, correspondence | `students/<name>/` — start at `students/README.md` |
| Voice for anything a student or prospect reads | `wiki/teaching-voice.md` |
| Was it sent? (every outbound draft/send) | `wiki/send-ledger.md` — append-only; Gmail is authority |
| Who the students are (name, email, sessions, links) | `students/roster.generated.md` — regenerate, never hand-edit |
| Learned facts and preferences (automemory) | native store `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/memory/` + `MEMORY.md` index |
| Skill / agent catalog | `skills/INDEX.md`, `agents/` |
| Systems Jay 2 can reach (registry) | `connections.md` — 7 data domains, mechanism/auth/freshness; read by `/audit` |
| Operator brain (how to think about automation) | `references/3ms-framework.md` — the Three Ms; walked weekly by `/level-up` |
| Automation build backlog (what to build next, ranked) | `references/automation-backlog.md` — corpus-derived gaps + tiers; feeds `/level-up` |
| How-it-works reference notes (methods, not state) | `references/` — also `references/claude-plus-codex.md`, `references/obsidian-command-center.md`, `references/second-brain-levels.md` |
| Web research and Firecrawl agents | `references/firecrawl.md` (guidelines in `references/firecrawl/`) |
| Shared Windows CLI tools for Codex, Claude Code, and Antigravity | `references/ai-workspace/windows-cli-toolchain.md` — installed paths, PATH refresh, and usage for `uv`, `yt-dlp`, FFmpeg, and Deno |
| Immutable source evidence · intake queue | `raw/sources/` · `raw/inbox/` — one inbox, one archive |
| Archived web clips, with provenance + hashes | `raw/sources/clips/<slug>/` — clip + `PROVENANCE.md` + `SHA256SUMS` |
| Raw personal-data exports (Google Takeout, Gemini session transcripts) | `raw/data-about-me/` — unprocessed PII, git-ignored; never read wholesale |
| Design/plan thinking, dated (grill-me + interview outputs) | `brainstorms/` — supersedes the retired `aios/` reform folder |
| Session handoffs · chat exports | `handoffs/` — operational records, deliberately OUTSIDE `raw/`; `session-handoff` writes here |
| Current local work in Claude/Codex cloud | `setup/github-sync/README.md`; private `codex/local-sync/<machine>/<checkout>` branches and generated `.cloud-sync/` metadata/conversation exports. Cloud edits use separate task branches. |
| External teardowns and research, dated | `research/` |
| Web-clipped articles (Obsidian Web Clipper) | `raw/inbox/` — intake queue; `ingest` copies and hash-verifies clips into `raw/sources/clips/`; processed originals stay until removal is authorized, so inbox presence alone does not mean unprocessed |
| Application code | `../ai-os-projects/lsatu/` — own repo, own instructions, own remote |
| Other projects (life-path, …) | `../ai-os-projects/<name>/` — each with its own `CLAUDE.md` + `.claude/` |

Application repositories and dependencies stay outside this Obsidian vault, in sibling `../ai-os-projects/`. Do not create a junction from the vault back into that directory.

## Retrieval order

1. `wiki/hot.md` — current-session narrative and open threads.
2. `wiki/index.md` → the page (teaching knowledge: `corpus/index.md`; a student: their folder). Follow links; there is no semantic search and none is needed at this size.
3. The live system — the final freshness escalation.

**Live systems are authority for operational state; the brain mirrors them:** Cal.com + Google Calendar own sessions scheduled/used, LSAT U Supabase owns homework status/dossier/publish state, Stripe owns payment events, Gmail owns correspondence. On any disagreement, the backend wins. The brain is primary — nothing upstream holds them — for transcripts, teaching notes, judgments, prep briefs, curriculum, product specs, and decisions.

**Do not read the wiki unless the task needs it.** Retrieval costs context every turn. Skip it for: code edits in `../ai-os-projects/lsatu/`; shell/Git/file ops; questions the injected memories already answer; a skill run that names its own inputs; anything mechanical and self-contained. Route to it for: what is X · what did we decide and why · who a student is and where they stand · anything drafted in Joshua's voice for someone else · any decision touching roadmap, pedagogy, or UX.

**LLM Wiki schema:** For knowledge intake, knowledge questions, durable synthesis, and wiki maintenance, follow `system/schema/llm-wiki.md`. Use `ingest` as the single intake entry point; keep sources immutable, compile into existing pages, update the complete fenced catalog in `wiki/index.md`, and append changes to `wiki/log.md`. Preserve domain homes and live-system authority. Mechanical tasks retain the retrieval exceptions above.

**Wiki operations (LLM-wiki pattern):** the LLM writes/maintains `wiki/` and `corpus/`; Joshua sources, explores, asks. Raw sources are immutable — read, never modify. *Ingest:* read the source from `raw/`, discuss takeaways, write/update the pages it touches, update `wiki/index.md`, append to `wiki/log.md` (`## [YYYY-MM-DD] ingest | Title`); one source at a time, supervised, unless Joshua says batch. *Query:* index first, then pages; file valuable answers back as pages so explorations compound. *Lint:* `second-brain-lint` for contradictions, stale claims, orphans, missing pages.

## Voice

- **No em-dashes, ever.** Joshua never types them; a stray em-dash reads as AI and costs trust.
- **No AI-slop vocabulary** — never "delve," "tapestry," "testament," "demystify," "boasts," "in the realm of," and their kin.
- **Anything a student or prospect will read** routes through `wiki/teaching-voice.md` and is drafted, never sent, unless it is a pre-approved template.
- **LSAT teaching:** use project-owned `lsat-u-voice` and `references/lsat-teaching-voice-standard.md`; this Joshua-selected standard takes precedence over older vendor voice blends.
- **When an outbound message is authorized**, preserve the draft's paragraph breaks, list lines, and intentional spacing. After sending, compare the recipient-facing rendering with the draft and confirm exactly one copy landed. Report any mismatch; never resend automatically.
- **Lead with your recommendation, not a menu.** Be decisive; don't over-hedge.
- **Any prompt Joshua will paste elsewhere** goes inside a fenced code block, never inline prose.

## Tools, keys, and secrets

- **The entire secret model is `.env` + `.gitignore`.** The root `.env` (git-ignored — verify with `git check-ignore .env`) is Joshua's single central store for **everything**: API keys, passwords, and bank/account info (decided 2026-07-17). OAuth client secrets live in `~/.config/gws/`, outside the repo. There is no vault, no Keychain, no encrypted store (`records.sparsebundle` is dead) and none is wanted. Never paste a credential into chat — add it to `.env` directly in the editor.
- **Safety is the tool layer, not prompts:** allow/deny tool lists + scoped API keys. Jay 2 acts under its own credentials and never impersonates Joshua.
- **Connected live systems:** Cal.com, Google Calendar, LSAT U Supabase, Stripe, Gmail. Full registry in `connections.md`.
- **Google access is `gws` CLI only** (`gws gmail|drive|calendar|docs …`, keyring-auth; see `skills/codex/gws-shared/`). Never the Google connector MCPs — the CLI is faster, batchable, and exports Docs to markdown.
- **Shared Windows command tools:** use `uv`, `yt-dlp`, `ffmpeg`, `ffprobe`, `ffplay`, and `deno` from the Windows user PATH. For versions, locations, how to invoke them, or a stale terminal environment, read `references/ai-workspace/windows-cli-toolchain.md`. Check before reinstalling; restart the affected IDE when its inherited PATH is stale.
- **Government IDs stay outside this OS.** Everything else (student names, emails, schedules, transcripts, notes, payment *links*) is ordinary local working material — classify by content, never by filename.
- **Git remote is enabled.** `origin` is GitHub (`vnjkoxpl-42905/ai-os`, decided 2026-09-29). The outer repo never tracks `.env`, `../ai-os-projects/lsatu/` (own repo), runtime logs, or locks. Do not create gitlinks.
- **New laptop restore:** GitHub contains tracked ai-os files and skills. It deliberately excludes `.env`, `raw/`, app repos, credentials, and machine sessions. Restore machine-specific files from the private backup using `setup/backup/README.md` and its `README - START HERE.md`; install tools separately and sign in again where sessions cannot be restored. Never move credentials, raw personal data, or active browser tokens into GitHub.
- **Authorized cloud transfer (2026-10-07):** private sync branches also carry portable skills, memory and conversation exports. Joshua explicitly authorized credential transfer; implementation uses the authenticated encrypted package described in `setup/github-sync/README.md`; plaintext secrets and its unlock key stay outside GitHub and Drive. Cloud authentication and native browser sessions still require separate verification.

## Skills

Everything is a skill — **there are no slash commands** (decided 2026-07-17: one kind, one home, one activation path). The catalog at `skills/` and `agents/` is inert storage; nothing auto-loads. An item runs only once **activated by copy** (not symlink) into `.agents/skills/` (Claude: `.claude/skills/`). Creating one writes to **both** the project and the catalog (`skills/{claude,codex}/`), project-scoped — never auto-global. `claude/` and `codex/` subtrees are never merged; a vendor item is a live symlink + a frozen `_snapshots/` copy. `system/scripts/check-skills.sh` validates the model; `skills/INDEX.md` indexes it.

Active in this root. Run `pickup` only when Joshua explicitly asks for it. Do not run it automatically at session start. Use `wrapup` at the end of substantive sessions:

| Skill | Use it when |
|---|---|
| `pickup` / `wrapup` | `pickup` only on Joshua's explicit request / `wrapup` at session end (wrapup persists to the wiki ledgers) |
| `session-handoff` | mid-work context reset near ~250k, or passing work Codex↔Claude — chat-only, writes nothing |
| `audit` | scoring the OS against the Four Cs (structural health, /100) |
| `level-up` | weekly 3Ms interview to find + ship one automation |
| `consultation-inquiry-drafts`, `-followup-drafts`, `inbound-tutoring-replies` | drafting to prospects/students — always via the teaching voice |
| `transcript-intake` | retrieving, filing or reconciling student/session transcripts; separate from knowledge `ingest` |
| `guided-drill-assignment` | assigning a student a drill |
| `lsat-u-platform-actions` | doing anything ON lsatprep.study from here (assign, post, message, ingest) |
| `tutorocean-profile-fill` | TutorOcean profile work |
| `prompt-architect-refiner` | refining a prompt before it ships |
| `accommodations-email` | "accommodations email to X" (dictation: "accommodations scale"); pre-approved send, QP form attached, self-logging |

When a skill run exposes a gap, improve the skill before moving on. Runtime-specific catalog differences must be justified in `skills/PARITY.md`; undeclared differences are defects.

## Guardrails

- **95% rule.** Make no changes until you are 95% confident what Joshua wants. Ask until you are.
- **Lovable credits require confirmation.** Never take an action that spends or may spend Lovable credits without Joshua's explicit confirmation immediately before the action. Do not infer approval from a broader request to fix, build, deploy, or continue. Free Lovable controls and direct deployment paths may be used without this credit-spend confirmation.
- **Lovable Cloud deploys through Lovable.** LSAT U currently uses a Lovable Cloud-managed backend. Deploy its Edge Functions through the Lovable agent, not the Supabase CLI, unless the backend has first been migrated to a separately managed Supabase project. Confirm this route with Joshua before starting a Lovable deployment.
- **Root stays thin.** Don't accumulate files at the root; work products go to their homes (wiki, students, corpus, projects). Anything left at root gets filed before the session ends.
- **Delegate bulk work.** Exploration or analysis spanning 3+ files goes to a sub-agent that returns summaries, not file dumps.
- **Shell ergonomics.** Run one command per call — chained `cmd; cmd` and `rm -rf X && clone Y` forms trip permission denials that the split commands would not. Never poll with `sleep`. Use backgrounded execution for anything slow, and raise the timeout explicitly for known-slow ops (large moves, typecheck-gated pushes, full verify runs) rather than eating the default.
- **Search before creating.** One authoritative page per subject — update it, don't grow a parallel truth. `wiki/index.md` catalogs every page and updates on every ingest.
- **Preserve provenance and source hashes.** Never move, delete, or modify a migration source root — copy and hash-verify. Never follow a source symlink outside its registered root.
- **Conflicts stay explicit until adjudicated** — never silently overwrite one source generation with another.
- **Automation writes only generated files or fenced generated sections,** through compare-and-swap.
- **Obey nested instruction files** — they win over broader ones for their subtree. LSATU's recovered `CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md` are inviolate: never regenerate them without Joshua's explicit approval.
- **Memory = native automemory.** "Remember that…" writes one fact per file to the native store; `MEMORY.md` indexes it. This file is the rules; memory is learned facts — never mix them. Transcripts and raw captures never auto-promote.
- **Append meaningful system changes** to `wiki/log.md`; log locked decisions to `decisions/log.md`.

## Dual-runtime (Codex + Claude)

- Begin at the Git-root `AGENTS.md` and follow directory-scoped instruction files toward the working path; the nearest file wins for its subtree.
- Keep root instructions inside the runtime byte budget. Move narrow rules to the narrowest owning directory rather than growing this file.
- Activated skills are **copies** in `.agents/skills/` (from `skills/codex/`); agent definitions convert to `.codex/agents/*.toml` with the instructions in `developer_instructions`.
- Claude reads `CLAUDE.md` and never this file. Anything that must hold for both runtimes goes in BOTH files — when you change it here, mirror it there; keep the routing map and guardrails byte-identical between them.
- Activation is unequal by design: Codex reads copies in `.agents/skills/`, Claude reads copies in `.claude/skills/`. Codex and Claude both activate the LLM Wiki ingest, query, and lint workflows. Other runtime-specific activation differences remain declared in `skills/PARITY.md`. Never have separate runtimes writing the same page concurrently.
- Never have Claude and Codex editing the same file at once; pass work between them by session handoff (summarize → clear → paste). At ~250k tokens, hand off rather than riding autocompact.

</INSTRUCTIONS>

All agents share the same directory. In detail:
- All agents have access to the same container and filesystem as you.
- All agents use the same current working directory.
- As a result, edits made by one agent are immediately visible to all other agents.

When calling `wait_agent`, prefer longer waits (minutes) to avoid busy polling.

There are 4 available concurrency slots, meaning that up to 4 agents can be active at once, including you.

Full-history forks (`fork_turns` omitted or `"all"`) inherit the parent model and reasoning effort and do not accept overrides. \'page\' : undefined} to={url(\'labs\')} onClick={closeForNavigation}><Dumbbell size={18}/>Drill Lab</Link>\\r\\n    <p>Tools</p><Link aria-current={view === \'tools\' ? \'page\' : undefined} to={url(\'tools\')} onClick={closeForNavigation}><Bookmark size={18}/>Flashcards</Link>\\r\\n  </nav>;\\r\\n  return <>\\r\\n    <aside className=\\"oc-course-rail\\"><Link className=\\"oc-back\\" to=\\"/classroom\\"><ArrowLeft size={15}/>Classroom</Link><div className=\\"oc-brand\\">Conditional logic</div>{navigation}</aside>\\r\\n    <div className=\\"oc-mobile-nav\\"><Link to={url(\'lessons\')}>Conditional logic</Link><Sheet open={open} onOpenChange={setOpen}><SheetTrigger asChild><button type=\\"button\\"><Menu size={18}/>Course menu</button></SheetTrigger><SheetContent side=\\"left\\" className=\\"oc-course-drawer\\" aria-describedby={undefined} onCloseAutoFocus={event => {\\r\\n      if (!focusDestination.current) return;\\r\\n      event.preventDefault();\\r\\n      focusDestination.current = false;\\r\\n      requestAnimationFrame(() => {\\r\\n        const heading = document.querySelector<HTMLElement>(\'.oc-course-screen [data-course-heading] h1\');\\r\\n        if (heading) { heading.tabIndex = -1; heading.focus({ preventScroll: true }); }\\r\\n      });\\r\\n    }}><SheetTitle>Conditional logic</SheetTitle>{navigation}</SheetContent></Sheet></div>\\r\\n  </>;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction CourseCard({ step, url }: { step: OpeningCourseStep; url: OpeningCourseUrl }) {\\r\\n  const saved = useCourseProgress()?.steps[step.id];\\r\\n  const drill = step.type === \'drill\';\\r\\n  const count = drill ? getSourceWorksheetDrill(step.id)?.items.length : undefined;\\r\\n  const number = step.title.match(/^(Revisit )?Drill (\\\\d+)/);\\r\\n  return <li className={`oc-path-card${drill ? \' oc-drill-card\' : \'\'}`} data-progress={saved?.status ?? \'new\'}>\\r\\n    {number && <span className=\\"oc-card-num\\" aria-hidden=\\"true\\">{number[2].padStart(2, \'0\')}</span>}\\r\\n    <span className=\\"oc-label\\">{number?.[1] ? \'Revisit\' : drill ? {};\\r\\n  const done = steps.filter(step => saved[step.id]?.status === \'done\').length;\\r\\n  return <div className=\\"oc-tracker\\" role=\\"group\\" aria-label=\\"Your progress\\">\\r\\n    <div className=\\"oc-tracker-top\\"><span>Your progress</span><strong>{done} <small>of {steps.length} done</small></strong></div>\\r\\n    <div className=\\"oc-tracker-steps\\">{steps.map(step => <i key={step.id} data-progress={saved[step.id]?.status ?? \'new\'} data-type={step.type} title={step.title}/>)}</div>\\r\\n  </div>;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/** Marks a lesson started on arrival and done once its closing navigation is reached. */\\r\\nfunction LessonCompletion({ stepId }: { stepId: string }) {\\r\\n  const store = useCourseProgress();\\r\\n  const save = store?.save;\\r\\n  useEffect(() => {\\r\\n    if (!save) return;\\r\\n    save(stepId, {});\\r\\n    const end = document.querySelector(\'.oc-course-turn\');\\r\\n    if (!end || typeof IntersectionObserver === \'undefined\') return;\\r\\n    const observer = new IntersectionObserver(entries => {\\r\\n      if (entries.some(entry => entry.isIntersecting)) { save(stepId, { status: \'done\' }); observer.disconnect(); }\\r\\n    });\\r\\n    observer.observe(end);\\r\\n    return () => observer.disconnect();\\r\\n  }, [save, stepId]);\\r\\n  return null;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nexport function OpeningCourseScreen({ view = \'lessons\', run = false, url, steps = OPENING_COURSE_PATH, recordedPractice, storageScope, progressStore }: {\\r\\n  view?: string; run?: boolean; url: OpeningCourseUrl; steps?: OpeningCourseStep[]; recordedPractice?: ReactNode; storageScope?: string; progressStore?: CourseProgressStore;\\r\\n}) {\\r\\n  const device = useDeviceCourseProgress(storageScope);\\r\\n  return <CourseProgressProvider store={progressStore ?? device}><CourseScreen view={view} run={run} url={url} steps={steps} recordedPractice={recordedPractice} storageScope={storageScope}/></CourseProgressProvider>;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction CourseScreen({ view, run, url, steps, recordedPractice, storageScope }: {\\r\\n  view: string; run: boolean; url: OpeningCourseUrl; steps: OpeningCourseStep[]; recordedPractice?: ReactNode; storageScope?: string;\\r\\n}) {\\r\\n  const current = steps.find(step => step.id === view);\\r\\n  const [visited, setVisited] = useState<string[]>([]);\\r\\n  const root = useRef<HTMLDivElement>(null);\\r\\n  useEffect(() => {\\r\\n    if (current && (current.type === \'note\' || run)) setVisited(prior => prior.includes(view) ? prior : [...prior, view]);\\r\\n    requestAnimationFrame(() => {\\r\\n      const heading = root.current?.querySelector<HTMLElement>(\'[data-course-heading] h1\');\\r\\n      if (heading) heading.tabIndex = -1;\\r\\n      heading?.focus({ preventScroll: true });\\r\\n      const arrival = root.current?.querySelector<HTMLElement>(current?.type === \'drill\' && run ? steps.filter(step => step.type === \'drill\') : steps;\\r\\n  const isIndex = [\'lessons\', \'labs\', \'practice\'].includes(view);\\r\\n  return <div ref={root} className={`oc-course-screen${view === \'foundation-practice\' ? \' d1-screen\' : \'\'}`}>\\r\\n    {isIndex && <div className=\\"oc-course-index\\">\\r\\n      <div data-course-heading><PageHeader noBloom eyebrow={view === \'lessons\' ? \'Introduction\' : \'Practice\'} title={view === \'lessons\' ? \'Conditional reasoning.\' : view === \'labs\' ? \'Make the move yourself.\' : \'Practice the reasoning.\'}/></div>\\r\\n      <CourseTracker steps={list}/>\\r\\n      {view === \'practice\' && recordedPractice}\\r\\n      <ol className=\\"oc-course-path\\">{list.map(step => <CourseCard key={step.id} step={step} url={url}/>)}</ol>\\r\\n      {!list.length && <p>No opening lessons are available in this work.</p>}\\r\\n    </div>}\\r\\n    {view === \'tools\' && <FlashcardTools url={url} allowed={[...(steps.some(step => step.unitId === \'orientation\' || step.unitId === \'sufficient-necessary\') ? \'oc-course-note\' : \'oc-drill-run-header\'}>\\r\\n      <div data-course-heading>{current.type === \'note\' ? <header className=\\"mb-10\\">\\r\\n        <h1 className=\\"font-mc-serif text-ink leading-[0.94] italic text-[clamp(3.1rem,7vw,6rem)] font-medium tracking-[-0.06em]\\">{current.title}</h1>\\r\\n        <p className=\\"font-mc-sans text-body-prose mt-4 text-ink-soft max-w-[60ch] leading-relaxed\\">{current.id === \'necessary\' ? <strong>{current.description}</strong> : current.description}</p>\\r\\n      </header> : <header><p className=\\"oc-label\\">Practice</p><h1>{current.title}</h1></header>}</div>\\r\\n      {current.type === \'drill\' && <Link className=\\"oc-back oc-drill-back\\" to={url(view)}>Back to instructions</Link>}\\r\\n    </div>}\\r\\n    <div className=\\"oc-course-bodies\\">{steps.filter(step => visited.includes(step.id)).map(step => <div key={step.id} hidden={step.id !== view || (step.type === \'drill\' && !run)} className={step.id === \'foundation-practice\' ? \'oc-course-body d1-body\' : \'oc-course-body co-opening cr-sequence\'}><CourseFlashcardUrl.Provider value={url}>{getSourceWorksheetDrill(step.id) ? <SourceWorksheetDrill setId={step.id} run startUrl={url(step.id, true)} storageScope={storageScope}/> : <OpeningCourseContent id={step.id}/>}</CourseFlashcardUrl.Provider></div>)}</div>\\r\\n    {current?.type === \'note\' && <LessonCompletion key={current.id} stepId={current.id}/>}\\r\\n    {current && (current.type === \'note\' || run) && <nav className=\\"oc-course-turn\\" aria-label=\\"Course navigation\\">{prior ? <Link to={url(prior.id)}><span>Previous {prior.type === \'note\' ? \'lesson\' : \'drill\'}</span><strong>{prior.title}</strong></Link> : <Link to={url(\'lessons\')}>All lessons</Link>}{next ? <Link to={url(next.id)}><span>Next {next.type === \'note\' ? \'lesson\' : \'drill\'}</span><strong>{next.title}<ArrowRight size={18}/></strong></Link> : <Link to={url(\'lessons\')}>Return to lessons<ArrowRight size={18}/></Link>}</nav>}\\r\\n    {!isIndex && view !== \'tools\' && !current && <div className=\\"oc-course-note\\"><h1>This lesson is not available here.</h1><Link to={url(\'lessons\')}>Return to lessons</Link></div>}\\r\\n  </div>;\\r\\n}\\r\\n"}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"7b9402","wall_time_seconds":0.601978,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":1891,"output":"import { useEffect, useRef, useState } from \'react\';\\r\\nimport { Check, Minus } from \'lucide-react\';\\r\\nimport { Pill, PulseWord } from \'./ContrapositiveVisuals\';\\r\\nimport { QuickQuiz, type QuizItem } from \'./SufficientQuiz\';\\r\\nimport \'./four-situations.css\';\\r\\n\\r\\ntype Situation = { key: string; idea: string; tone: \'amber\' | \'teal\'; present: boolean; follows: string; example: string; tag: string; settled: boolean };\\r\\n\\r\\n// Source: the activation chart in raw/sources/conditional-reasoning-voice-standard/conditional_reasoning_transcription.html.\\r\\n// Same four rows in the same order; the example is original.\\r\\nconst situations: Situation[] = [\\r\\n  { key: \'sufficient-present\', idea: \'Sufficient\', tone: \'amber\', present: true, follows: \'The necessary condition must be present.\', example: \'The pool is open, so a lifeguard must be on duty.\', tag: \'The original rule\', settled: true },\\r\\n  { key: \'necessary-absent\', idea: \'Necessary\', tone: \'teal\', present: false, follows: \'The sufficient condition can’t be present.\', example: \'No lifeguard is on duty, so the pool can’t be open.\', tag: \'The contrapositive\', settled: true },\\r\\n  { key: \'sufficient-absent\', idea: \'Sufficient\', tone: \'amber\', present: false, follows: \'Nothing!\', example: \'The pool is closed. A lifeguard may or may not be on duty.\', tag: \'The rule is silent\', settled: false },\\r\\n  { key: \'necessary-present\', idea: \'Necessary\', tone: \'teal\', present: true, follows: \'Nothing!\', example: \'A lifeguard is on duty. The pool may or may not be open.\', tag: \'The rule is silent\', settled: false },\\r\\n];\\r\\n\\r\\n/** The four-situation chart. Rows rise in one after another when the chart scrolls into view;\\r\\n *  the two that settle something get a green check, the two that settle nothing go quiet. */\\r\\nfunction SituationChart() {\\r\\n  const ref = useRef<HTMLTableElement>(null);\\r\\n  const [inView, setInView] = useState(typeof IntersectionObserver === \'undefined\');\\r\\n  useEffect(() => {\\r\\n    const node = ref.current;\\r\\n    if (!node || typeof IntersectionObserver === \'undefined\') return;\\r\\n    const observer = new IntersectionObserver(entries => { if (entries.some(entry => entry.isIntersecting)) { setInView(true); observer.disconnect(); } }, { threshold: 0.3 });\\r\\n    observer.observe(node);\\r\\n    return () => observer.disconnect();\\r\\n  }, []);\\r\\n  return <table ref={ref} className=\\"fs-chart\\" data-in-view={inView}>\\r\\n    <caption className=\\"fs-chart-rule\\"><span>The rule</span>If the pool is open, then a lifeguard is on duty.</caption>\\r\\n    <thead><tr><th scope=\\"col\\">Situation</th><th scope=\\"col\\">What follows?</th><th scope=\\"col\\">The pool rule</th></tr></thead>\\r\\n    <tbody>\\r\\n      {situations.map((row, index) => <tr key={row.key} data-settled={row.settled} style={{ animationDelay: `${index * 0.14}s` }}>\\r\\n        <th scope=\\"row\\" data-label=\\"Situation\\"><span className=\\"fs-situation\\"><Pill text={row.idea} tone={row.tone} negated={false}/>{\' \'}<span>{row.present ? \'present\' : \'absent\'}</span></span></th>\\r\\n        <td data-label=\\"What follows?\\"><span className=\\"fs-follows\\">{row.settled ? <Check size={18} strokeWidth={3} aria-hidden=\\"true\\"/> : <Minus size={18} strokeWidth={3} aria-hidden=\\"true\\"/>}{row.follows}</span></td>\\r\\n        <td data-label=\\"The pool rule\\"><span className=\\"fs-tag\\">{row.tag}</span><span className=\\"fs-example\\">{row.example}</span></td>\\r\\n      </tr>)}\\r\\n    </tbody>\\r\\n  </table>;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nconst quiz: QuizItem[] = [\\r\\n  {\\r\\n    rule: \'If a library book is overdue, the borrower gets a reminder text.\',\\r\\n    question: \'Sam’s book is overdue. What follows?\',\\r\\n    options: [\\r\\n      { text: \'Nothing. Sam may or may not get a text.\', reason: \'An overdue book is the sufficient condition, so the rule applies. Sam gets the text.\' },\\r\\n      { text: \'Sam gets a reminder text.\' },\\r\\n      { text: \'Sam returns the book soon.\', reason: \'The rule says nothing about returning the book.\' },\\r\\n    ],\\r\\n    correct: 1,\\r\\n    answer: \'Sam’s book is overdue, so Sam gets a reminder text.\',\\r\\n  },\\r\\n  {\\r\\n    rule: \'If a library book is overdue, the borrower gets a reminder text.\',\\r\\n    question: \'Lena got a reminder text. What follows about her book?\',\\r\\n    options: [\\r\\n      { text: \'Her book is overdue.\', reason: \'The text could be about something else, like a hold that is ready.\' },\\r\\n      { text: \'Her book is not overdue.\', reason: \'A text doesn’t rule out an overdue book either.\' },\\r\\n      { text: \'Nothing. Her book may or may not be overdue.\' },\\r\\n    ],\\r\\n    correct: 2,\\r\\n    answer: \'A reminder text is the necessary condition, so it tells you nothing about Lena’s book.\',\\r\\n  },\\r\\n  {\\r\\n    rule: \'If a library book is overdue, the borrower gets a reminder text.\',\\r\\n    question: \'Omar got no reminder text. What follows about his book?\',\\r\\n    options: [\\r\\n      { text: \'His book is not overdue.\' },\\r\\n      { text: \'Nothing. His book may or may not be overdue.\', reason: \'An overdue book would have brought a text. No text rules that out.\' },\\r\\n      { text: \'His book is overdue.\', reason: \'An overdue book would have brought a text, and Omar got none.\' },\\r\\n    ],\\r\\n    correct: 0,\\r\\n    answer: \'Omar got no reminder text, so his book isn’t overdue.\',\\r\\n  },\\r\\n  {\\r\\n    rule: \'If a library book is overdue, the borrower gets a reminder text.\',\\r\\n    question: \'Priya’s book is not overdue. What follows?\',\\r\\n    options: [\\r\\n      { text: \'She gets a reminder text.\', reason: \'Nothing in the rule requires a text here.\' },\\r\\n      { text: \'Nothing. She may or may not get a text.\' },\\r\\n      { text: \'She gets no reminder text.\', reason: \'She could still get a text for another reason.\' },\\r\\n    ],\\r\\n    correct: 1,\\r\\n    answer: \'The rule only speaks when a book is overdue, so it says nothing about Priya.\',\\r\\n  },\\r\\n];\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction Section({ title, children }: { title: string; children: React.ReactNode }) {\\r\\n  return <section className=\\"co-section\\"><div className=\\"co-section-heading\\"><h2>{title}</h2></div>{children}</section>;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nexport function FourSituationsNote() {\\r\\n  return <>\\r\\n    <Section title=\\"All a rule gives you\\">\\r\\n      <p className=\\"ct-definition\\"><strong>A conditional tells you two things: the original rule and its contrapositive. That is all it tells you.</strong></p>\\r\\n      <p>Take this rule: <strong>If the pool is open, then a lifeguard is on duty.</strong></p>\\r\\n      <p>The original: if the pool is open, a lifeguard must be on duty. The contrapositive: <strong>if no lifeguard is on duty, the pool can’t be open.</strong> The rule says nothing else.</p>\\r\\n    </Section>\\r\\n    <Section title=\\"Don’t fall for the necessary condition\\">\\r\\n      <p>The LSAT will give you a rule, then tell you the necessary condition is present. A lifeguard is on duty! You’ll want to conclude the pool is open. Don’t. The lifeguard could be there for a training session with the pool closed.</p>\\r\\n      <p>When the necessary condition is present, <PulseWord>the rule tells you nothing.</PulseWord> Set it aside.</p>\\r\\n    </Section>\\r\\n    <Section title=\\"Four situations\\">\\r\\n      <p>Any fact about a rule puts you in one of four situations. Two of them settle something. Two settle nothing.</p>\\r\\n      <SituationChart/>\\r\\n    </Section>\\r\\n    <Section title=\\"Ask where the fact lands\\">\\r\\n      <p>When a rule comes with a fact, check which side the fact is about. Sufficient present or necessary absent, and the rule settles something. Sufficient absent or necessary present, and it settles nothing, so look for help somewhere else in the argument.</p>\\r\\n    </Section>\\r\\n  </>;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nexport function FourSituationsQuiz() {\\r\\n  return <QuickQuiz items={quiz} idPrefix=\\"fs\\"/>;\\r\\n}\\r\\n"}'}]

Returns null outside a course (isolated\\r\\n * component tests), where drills fall back to their own device storage.\\r\\n */\\r\\nexport function useCourseStep(stepId: string | undefined) {\\r\\n  const store = useContext(CourseProgressContext);\\r\\n  const save = store?.save;\\r\\n  const record = useCallback((patch: CourseStepPatch) => { if (stepId) save?.(stepId, patch); }, [save, stepId]);\\r\\n  if (!store || !stepId) return null;\\r\\n  return { saved: store.steps[stepId], record };\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/** Device-only store for the preview harness and signed-out use; the course route uses the account. */\\r\\nexport function useDeviceCourseProgress(scope = \'preview\'): CourseProgressStore {\\r\\n  const key = `conditional-course-progress:${scope}`;\\r\\n  const [steps, setSteps] = useState<Record<string, CourseStepProgress>>(() => {\\r\\n    try { return JSON.parse(localStorage.getItem(key) ?? \'{}\') ?? {}; } catch { return {}; }\\r\\n  });\\r\\n  const save = useCallback((stepId: string, patch: CourseStepPatch) => setSteps(previous => {\\r\\n    const prior = previous[stepId];\\r\\n    const status = prior?.status === \'done\' ? Click Step 1.\', \'Fuel is the requirement, even though it comes first. Now click Step 2.\', \'Fuel still goes after “then.” Its place in the sentence doesn’t decide its role.\'],\\r\\n};\\r\\n\\r\\n/** Step 1 sweeps a highlight across the requirement in the sentence. Step 2 drops both ideas into an if-then frame. */\\r\\nfunction FindTheRequirement({ label, parts, ifIdea, thenIdea, result, captions }: FindDemo) {\\r\\n  const [stage, setStage] = useState(0);\\r\\n  return <div className=\\"ns-find\\" data-stage={stage} role=\\"group\\" aria-label={label}>\\r\\n    <p className=\\"ns-find-rule\\"><span>The rule</span>{parts.map((part, index) => typeof part === \'string\' ? part : <mark key={index} className=\\"ns-mark\\" data-role={part.role} data-lit={part.role === \'necessary\' ? stage >= 1 : stage >= 2}>{part.text}</mark>)}</p>\\r\\n    <div className=\\"ns-frame\\" data-show={stage >= 2} aria-hidden={stage < 2}>\\r\\n      <span className=\\"ns-frame-word\\">If</span><span className=\\"ns-slot\\" data-role=\\"sufficient\\">{ifIdea}</span>\\r\\n      <span className=\\"ns-frame-word\\">then</span><span className=\\"ns-slot\\" data-role=\\"necessary\\">{thenIdea}</span>\\r\\n    </div>\\r\\n    {stage >= 2 && <p className=\\"ns-find-result\\"><span>Read it as</span>{result}</p>}\\r\\n    <p className=\\"ns-find-caption\\" role=\\"status\\">{captions[stage]}</p>\\r\\n    <div className=\\"ct-flip-controls\\">\\r\\n      <button type=\\"button\\" className=\\"ct-step\\" data-ready={stage === 0} data-done={stage >= 1} disabled={stage !== 0} onClick={() => setStage(1)}><b aria-hidden=\\"true\\">{stage >= 1 ? <Check size={16} strokeWidth={3}/> : 1}</b>Step 1. Click here to put it after “then”</button>\\r\\n      {stage > 0 && <button type=\\"button\\" className=\\"ct-flip-reset\\" onClick={() => setStage(0)}><RotateCcw size={15} aria-hidden=\\"true\\"/>Start over</button>}\\r\\n    </div>\\r\\n  </div>;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nconst groupTwoWords = [\'only\', \'only if\', \'only when\', \'only where\', \'always\', \'requires\', \'must\'];\\r\\n\\r\\n/** The one look-alike that fails beside the reading that holds. */\\r\\nfunction RequiredNotEnough() {\\r\\n  const cards: { verdict: \'wrong\' | \'right\'; how: string; left: string; leftTone: \'amber\' | \'teal\'; right: string; rightTone: \'amber\' | \'teal\' }[] = [\\r\\n    { verdict: \'wrong\', how: \'Treat the requirement as enough.\', left: \'Student\', leftTone: \'teal\', right: \'Enrolled\', rightTone: \'amber\' },\\r\\n    { verdict: \'right\', how: \'Enrollment guarantees the requirement.\', left: \'Enrolled\', leftTone: \'amber\', right: \'Student\', rightTone: \'teal\' },\\r\\n  ];\\r\\n  return <div className=\\"ns-cards\\">{cards.map(card => <article key={card.verdict} className=\\"ct-move\\" data-verdict={card.verdict}>\\r\\n    <p className=\\"ct-move-badge\\">{card.verdict === \'right\' ? <Check size={16} strokeWidth={3} aria-hidden=\\"true\\"/> : <X size={16} strokeWidth={3} aria-hidden=\\"true\\"/>}{card.verdict === \'right\' ? \'Right\' : \'Wrong\'}</p>\\r\\n    <p className=\\"ct-move-how\\">{card.how}</p>\\r\\n    <p className=\\"ct-move-diagram\\" aria-label={`${card.left} → ${card.right}`}><Pill text={card.left} tone={card.leftTone} negated={false}/><span aria-hidden=\\"true\\">→</span><Pill text={card.right} tone={card.rightTone} negated={false}/></p>\\r\\n  </article>)}</div>;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nconst quiz: QuizItem[] = [\\r\\n  {\\r\\n    rule: \'Every bike in the race has working brakes.\',\\r\\n    question: \'Which idea is required?\',\\r\\n    options: [\\r\\n      { text: \'In the race\', tone: \'amber\', reason: \'Being in the race guarantees the brakes. It isn’t the requirement.\' },\\r\\n      { text: \'Working brakes\', tone: \'teal\' },\\r\\n      { text: \'Any bike\', tone: \'amber\', reason: \'The rule only covers bikes in the race.\' },\\r\\n    ],\\r\\n    correct: 1,\\r\\n    answer: \'If a bike is in the race, then it has working brakes.\',\\r\\n  },\\r\\n  {\\r\\n    rule: \'Every bike in the race has working brakes.\',\\r\\n    question: \'Kai’s bike has working brakes. Is it in the race?\',\\r\\n    options: [\\r\\n      { text: \'Yes, it’s in the race.\', reason: \'Brakes are required, not enough. It has to be met, but meeting it might not be enough.</strong></p>\\r\\n      <p>You have to be a student to be enrolled in law school. Being a student is <strong>necessary</strong> for law-school enrollment. But being a student doesn’t prove you’re enrolled. You could be studying history.</p>\\r\\n    </Section>\\r\\n    <Section title=\\"Where the certainty is\\">\\r\\n      <p><strong>The necessary condition is what you can guarantee will occur if the sufficient condition happens.</strong></p>\\r\\n      <p>Look at the name. It\'s called necessary because it is necessary. If someone is enrolled in law school, being a student has to be true.</p>\\r\\n      <p>The sufficient condition opens the door. Once it\'s open, the necessary condition is guaranteed to be on the other side. That makes it <PulseWord>the place of certainty.</PulseWord> It\'s what you can prove.</p>\\r\\n    </Section>\\r\\n    <Section title=\\"Watch the lesson\\">\\r\\n      <SufficientConditionFilm assets={necessaryConditionFilms} title=\\"Why the requirement is not enough\\" ariaLabel=\\"Short explanation of necessity\\"/>\\r\\n    </Section>\\r\\n    <Section title=\\"How to find it\\">\\r\\n      <ol className=\\"ct-moves\\">\\r\\n        <li><strong>Step 1: Find the requirement.</strong> Ask what has to be true if the other idea happens.</li>\\r\\n        <li><strong>Step 2: Put it after “then.”</strong> The idea that requires it goes after “if.”</li>\\r\\n      </ol>\\r\\n      <FindTheRequirement {...lawSchool}/>\\r\\n      <p>Same two steps when the rule names the requirement first:</p>\\r\\n      <FindTheRequirement {...fuel}/>\\r\\n    </Section>\\r\\n    <Section title=\\"Words that mark the requirement\\">\\r\\n      <p>In the fuel rule, <strong>necessary</strong> marked the requirement. These Group 2 words and phrases mark it too:</p>\\r\\n      <ul className=\\"ns-words\\">{groupTwoWords.map(word => <li key={word}>{word}</li>)}</ul>\\r\\n      <p>The indicator is the word. The necessary condition is the whole idea it points to.</p>\\r\\n    </Section>\\r\\n    <Section title=\\"What it is not\\">\\r\\n      <p>A requirement is not a guarantee. Wrong answer choices count on you forgetting that.</p>\\r\\n      <RequiredNotEnough/>\\r\\n    </Section>\\r\\n    <Section title=\\"Picture it on its own\\">\\r\\n      <p>When you find the requirement, picture it true while the other idea is false. A history student. A car with fuel and a dead battery. If that picture fits the rule, you have a requirement, and it proves nothing by itself.</p>\\r\\n    </Section>\\r\\n    <section className=\\"co-section\\"><div className=\\"co-section-heading\\"><PopQuizTitle/></div><QuickQuiz items={quiz} idPrefix=\\"nq\\"/></section>\\r\\n  </>;\\r\\n}\\r\\n"}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"0d98a5","wall_time_seconds":0.5615134,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":1255,"output":"export type OpeningCourseStep = {\\r\\n  id: string;\\r\\n  type: \'note\' | \'drill\';\\r\\n  title: string;\\r\\n  description: string;\\r\\n  alsoAvailableIn?: Array<\'orientation\' | \'sufficient-necessary\' | \'group-1\' | \'group-2\'>;\\r\\n  unitId: \'orientation\' | \'sufficient-necessary\' | \'group-1\' | \'group-2\';\\r\\n};\\r\\n\\r\\n// Reading destinations are not legacy completion IDs. [{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 11.4 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"d3ef3a","wall_time_seconds":0.4549334,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":1419,"output":"import { useState } from \'react\';\\r\\nimport { Check, RotateCcw, X } from \'lucide-react\';\\r\\nimport { Pill } from \'./ContrapositiveVisuals\';\\r\\n\\r\\nexport type QuizOption = { text: string; tone?: \'amber\' | \'teal\'; reason?: string };\\r\\nexport type QuizItem = { rule: string; question: string; options: QuizOption[]; correct: number; answer: string };\\r\\n\\r\\nconst items: QuizItem[] = [\\r\\n  {\\r\\n    rule: \'Every applicant invited to an interview receives an email.\',\\r\\n    question: \'Which idea is enough to guarantee the email?\',\\r\\n    options: [\\r\\n      { text: \'Invited to an interview\', tone: \'amber\' },\\r\\n      { text: \'Any applicant\', tone: \'amber\', reason: \'The rule covers invited applicants, not every applicant.\' },\\r\\n      { text: \'Receives an email\', tone: \'teal\', reason: \'That is what the rule guarantees, not what guarantees it.\' },\\r\\n    ],\\r\\n    correct: 0,\\r\\n    answer: \'If an applicant is invited to an interview, then the applicant gets an email.\',\\r\\n  },\\r\\n  {\\r\\n    rule: \'Whenever it rains on match day, the match is cancelled.\',\\r\\n    question: \'Which idea is enough to cancel the match?\',\\r\\n    options: [\\r\\n      { text: \'It is match day\', tone: \'amber\', reason: \'Match day alone is not enough. The rule needs rain too.\' },\\r\\n      { text: \'It rains on match day\', tone: \'amber\' },\\r\\n      { text: \'The match is cancelled\', tone: \'teal\', reason: \'That is the result, not the idea that guarantees it.\' },\\r\\n    ],\\r\\n    correct: 1,\\r\\n    answer: \'If it rains on match day, then the match is cancelled.\',\\r\\n  },\\r\\n  {\\r\\n    rule: \'Every applicant invited to an interview receives an email.\',\\r\\n    question: \'An applicant is not invited. What does the rule say about an email?\',\\r\\n    options: [\\r\\n      { text: \'No email.\', reason: \'The rule never says only invited applicants get emails.\' },\\r\\n      { text: \'Nothing. They might or might not get one.\' },\\r\\n      { text: \'They get one anyway.\', reason: \'The rule does not promise that either.\' },\\r\\n    ],\\r\\n    correct: 1,\\r\\n    answer: \'If an applicant is not invited, the rule says nothing about an email.\',\\r\\n  },\\r\\n  {\\r\\n    rule: \\"If you\'re enrolled in law school, you\'re a student.\\",\\r\\n    question: \'Dana is a student. What does the rule say about law school?\',\\r\\n    options: [\\r\\n      { text: \'She is enrolled.\', reason: \'Being a student is not enough to prove enrollment.\' },\\r\\n      { text: \'She is not enrolled.\', reason: \'The rule does not rule enrollment out.\' },\\r\\n      { text: \'Nothing. She might be studying something else.\' },\\r\\n    ],\\r\\n    correct: 2,\\r\\n    answer: \'If someone is a student, the rule says nothing about law school.\',\\r\\n  },\\r\\n];\\r\\n\\r\\n/** Four questions, one at a time, each answered with one tap. The sufficient-condition twin of the contrapositive quiz. */\\r\\nexport function SufficientQuiz() {\\r\\n  return <QuickQuiz items={items} idPrefix=\\"sq\\"/>;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/** One question at a time, one tap each. A wrong tap says \\"Not that one.\\" and why; the right tap gives the answer as a plain sentence. */\\r\\nexport function QuickQuiz({ items, idPrefix }: { items: QuizItem[]; idPrefix: string }) {\\r\\n  const [step, setStep] = useState(0);\\r\\n  const [wrong, setWrong] = useState<number[]>([]);\\r\\n  const [solved, setSolved] = useState<number | null>(null);\\r\\n  const item = items[step];\\r\\n  const last = step === items.length - 1;\\r\\n  const lastWrong = wrong.length ? item.options[wrong[wrong.length - 1]] : null;\\r\\n  const message = solved !== null ? item.answer : lastWrong ? `Not that one. ${lastWrong.reason}` : \'Pick the answer the rule supports.\';\\r\\n  const next = () => { setStep(value => value + 1); setWrong([]); setSolved(null); };\\r\\n  const restart = () => { setStep(0); setWrong([]); setSolved(null); };\\r\\n  return <div className=\\"ct-drill\\" data-step={step}>\\r\\n    <div className=\\"ct-drill-top\\">\\r\\n      <p className=\\"ct-picker-kicker\\">Your turn</p>\\r\\n      <p className=\\"ct-drill-count\\">Question {step + 1} of {items.length}<span className=\\"ct-drill-dots\\" aria-hidden=\\"true\\">{items.map((entry, index) => <i key={index} data-state={index < step ? Choose labels you can read back.</li><li><strong>Write the contrapositive.</strong> Reverse the sides and negate both complete conditions.</li><li><strong>Read both in English.</strong> Compare them with what the original sentence actually requires.</li></ol></div>\\r\\n      <p>The original and its contrapositive describe the same rule. If writing both helps you see that rule, write both. If you can read the contrapositive from the original when it is relevant, you needn\'t copy it merely to have a second line.</p>\\r\\n      <p>Read an arrow forward to use its stated direction. To read its contrapositive, start from the right condition\'s absence and work back to the left condition\'s absence. That is a different move from reading the arrow backward unchanged.</p>\\r\\n    </ReferenceStep>\\r\\n\\r\\n    <ReferenceStep number={10} label=\\"Practical diagramming questions\\" title=\\"Use writing to make your reasoning easier to check\\">\\r\\n      <div className=\\"cr-faq\\">\\r\\n        <details><summary>When should I diagram?</summary><p>While learning, write enough to make your choices visible. A difficult sentence, several rules or a connection you keep rereading can be a reason to put the rule on paper. With practice, you may hold a simple rule mentally. Use the writing to help the reasoning.</p></details>\\r\\n        <details><summary>How do I check whether my diagram is right?</summary><p>Read it back and compare the whole meaning. Did you keep each restriction? Does your arrow promise something the original leaves open? When a diagram is wrong, record the exact phrase and the mistake it produced. That gives you something specific to practice. Familiarity alone is not proof of correctness.</p></details>\\r\\n        <details><summary>What if writing takes time?</summary><p>Start by producing a correct, readable diagram. First make the rule accurate. Work on speed once you can explain why the arrow follows. </p></details>\\r\\n        <details><summary>Should I always write the contrapositive?</summary><p>You need to understand it every time. Write it when it helps you use the missing requirement, inspect your translation or connect rules. Otherwise, you may read that direction from the original. Extra notation should have a job.</p></details>\\r\\n        <aside className=\\"cr-training-note\\"><h3>Why this practice asks for both</h3><p>In the integrated drill, write or choose the original and the contrapositive. We\'re checking that you can produce both. Once that skill is reliable, a performance task need not require both lines for every rule.</p></aside>\\r\\n        <details><summary>How should I abbreviate the conditions?</summary><p>Use short labels that bring back the complete idea. L might mean the guest is on the list. Current pass may be clearer than a single letter if you\'re tracking several kinds of pass. Shortening should save writing without losing meaning. There is no fixed letter limit.</p></details>\\r\\n      </div>\\r\\n      <ReferenceQuestion prompt=\\"Only envelopes with a paid stamp go into this tray. Someone wrote Paid stamp → In tray. What needs repairing?\\" options={[\'The labels need to be longer.\', \'The arrow makes the requirement enough.\', \'Nothing; the first phrase always goes left.\']} correct={1} feedback={[\'Longer labels would not repair the direction. Only identifies the paid stamp as required for tray placement.\']}/>\\r\\n      <p>That error came from the language. Approval can appear before or after the indicator without changing that requirement. Publication therefore goes on the left and editor approval on the right.</p>\\r\\n      <p>When the wording still feels uncertain, we can test a proposed translation against an imagined case.</p>\\r\\n    </ReferenceStep>\\r\\n\\r\\n    <ReferenceStep number={12} label=\\"Check an interpretation\\" title=\\"Try the rule from the inside\\">\\r\\n      <p>A <strong>“Put yourself in it”</strong> lets you test what a proposed sufficient condition would prove. Lena meets the condition for permission, but we have no fact reporting her use of the cloakroom.\', \'The rule gives Lena permission because she is a guest with a ticket. Told to return isn\'t returned. Your conclusion should keep the meaning the rule actually gave you.</p>\\r\\n    </section>\\r\\n  </div>;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nexport function ConditionalReferenceSequence() { return conditionalReferenceContent(); }\\r\\n\\r\\n// A long stage can contain several reading pages without changing the source order.\\r\\nexport function conditionalReferencePages(): LessonPage[] {\\r\\n  return Children.toArray(conditionalReferenceContent().props.children).flatMap(node => {\\r\\n    if (!isValidElement<{number?: number; title?: string; children?: ReactNode; \'data-toc-title\'?: string}>(node)) return [];\\r\\n    if (node.props.number === 8) {\\r\\n      const parts = Children.toArray(node.props.children);\\r\\n      const sentenceMove = parts.findIndex(child => isValidElement<{children?: ReactNode}>(child) && child.type === \'h3\' && child.props.children === \'The rule can move. useCourseStep } from \'../courseProgress\';\\r\\nimport { advance, cardById, isFinished, newRound, pick, readDeckState, summarize, type DeckState } from \'../indicatorWordDeck\';\\r\\nimport { IndicatorCardBack } from \'./IndicatorCardBack\';\\r\\nimport \'./indicator-word-deck.css\';\\r\\n\\r\\nexport const INDICATOR_DECK_STEP = \'flashcards-indicators\';\\r\\nconst DEVICE_KEY = \'conditional-indicator-words-v1\';\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction readDevice(): unknown {\\r\\n  try { return JSON.parse(localStorage.getItem(DEVICE_KEY) ?? \'null\'); } catch { return null; }\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/** One word per card, both groups mixed. The student commits to a group, the card flips, and the back says which group it was. */\\r\\nexport function IndicatorWordDeck({ stepId = INDICATOR_DECK_STEP }: { stepId?: string }) {\\r\\n  const course = useCourseStep(stepId);\\r\\n  const record = course?.record;\\r\\n  const [state, setState] = useState<DeckState>(() => readDeckState(course?.saved?.state) ?? readDeckState(readDevice()) ?? newRound());\\r\\n  const next = useRef<HTMLButtonElement>(null);\\r\\n  const heading = useRef<HTMLHeadingElement>(null);\\r\\n  const finished = isFinished(state);\\r\\n  const touched = state.picked !== null || state.index > 0 || state.round > 1;\\r\\n\\r\\n  // Every answer is saved to the account, so the student can leave and pick up on the same card in the same order.\\r\\n  useEffect(() => {\\r\\n    if (touched) record?.({ state, done: Math.min(state.index + (state.picked !== null ? 1 : 0), state.queue.length), total: state.queue.length, ...(finished ? { status: \'done\' as const } : {}) });\\r\\n    try { localStorage.setItem(DEVICE_KEY, JSON.stringify(state)); } catch { /* the account copy still holds it */ }\\r\\n  }, [state, record, touched, finished]);\\r\\n  useEffect(() => { if (state.picked !== null) next.current?.focus({ preventScroll: true }); }, [state.picked]);\\r\\n  useEffect(() => { if (finished) heading.current?.focus({ preventScroll: true }); }, [finished]);\\r\\n\\r\\n  const progress = <div className=\\"iw-progress\\" role=\\"progressbar\\" aria-label=\\"Cards done\\" aria-valuemin={0} aria-valuemax={state.queue.length} aria-valuenow={Math.min(state.index, state.queue.length)}><span style={{ width: `${Math.min(state.index, state.queue.length) / state.queue.length * 100}%` }}/></div>;\\r\\n\\r\\n  if (finished) {\\r\\n    const summary = summarize(state);\\r\\n    return <section className=\\"iw-deck\\" aria-label=\\"Indicator word flashcards\\">\\r\\n      {progress}\\r\\n      <div className=\\"iw-summary\\">\\r\\n        <h2 ref={heading} tabIndex={-1}>{summary.right} right first time.</h2>\\r\\n        <p>{summary.missed === 0 ? \'No card needed a second look.\' : `${summary.missed} needed a second look.`}{summary.stillMissing.length > 0 && ` Still to practice: ${summary.stillMissing.join(\', \')}.`}</p>\\r\\n        {state.earlier.length > 0 && <p className=\\"iw-earlier\\">Earlier rounds: {state.earlier.map((r, i) => `Round ${i + 1}, ${r.right} of ${r.total}`).join(\' · \')}.</p>}\\r\\n        <button type=\\"button\\" className=\\"iw-primary\\" onClick={() => setState(previous => newRound(previous))}><RotateCcw size={17} aria-hidden=\\"true\\"/>Go again</button>\\r\\n      </div>\\r\\n    </section>;\\r\\n  }\\r\\n\\r\\n  const entry = state.queue[state.index];\\r\\n  const card = cardById(entry.id)!;\\r\\n  const answered = state.picked !== null;\\r\\n  const right = answered && state.picked === card.group;\\r\\n  const firstCount = indicatorWordCards.length;\\r\\n  const position = entry.retry ? `Second look ${state.index - firstCount + 1} of ${state.queue.length - firstCount}` : `Card ${state.index + 1} of ${firstCount}`;\\r\\n  const lastCard = state.index === state.queue.length - 1;\\r\\n  const choose = (group: IndicatorGroup) => setState(previous => pick(previous, group));\\r\\n\\r\\n  return <section className=\\"iw-deck\\" aria-label=\\"Indicator word flashcards\\">\\r\\n    {progress}\\r\\n    <p className=\\"iw-count\\">{position}</p>\\r\\n    <div className=\\"iw-stage\\" data-result={answered ? (right ? \'right\' : \'wrong\') : undefined}>\\r\\n      <div className=\\"iw-card\\" data-flipped={answered}>\\r\\n        <div className=\\"iw-face iw-front\\" aria-hidden={answered}>\\r\\n          <span className=\\"iw-eyebrow\\">Which group?</span>\\r\\n          <span className=\\"iw-word\\">{displayWord(card.word)}</span>\\r\\n        </div>\\r\\n        <div className=\\"iw-face iw-back\\" aria-hidden={!answered}>\\r\\n          <IndicatorCardBack card={card} verdict={right ? \'Correct\' : \'Not quite\'}/>\\r\\n        </div>\\r\\n      </div>\\r\\n    </div>\\r\\n    <p className=\\"iw-sr\\" role=\\"status\\">{answered ? `${right ? \'Correct\' : \'Not quite\'}. ${displayWord(card.word)}. ${groupSentence(card.group)} Put the idea on the ${groupSide(card.group)} side.` : \'\'}</p>\\r\\n    {answered\\r\\n      ? <button ref={next} type=\\"button\\" className=\\"iw-primary\\" onClick={() => setState(advance)}>{lastCard ? \'See my results\' : \'Next card\'}<ArrowRight size={17} aria-hidden=\\"true\\"/></button>\\r\\n      : <div className=\\"iw-choices\\" role=\\"group\\" aria-label={`Which group is ${card.word}?`}>\\r\\n        <button type=\\"button\\" onClick={() => choose(1)}>Group 1</button>\\r\\n        <button type=\\"button\\" onClick={() => choose(2)}>Group 2</button>\\r\\n      </div>}\\r\\n  </section>;\\r\\n}\\r\\n"}'}]
