click(`Group ${correct ? card.group : card.group === 1 ? 2 : 1}`); return card; };\\r\\n\\r\\nit(\'shows only the word, flips on a choice, and says the group in one line\', () => {\\r\\n  render(<IndicatorWordDeck/>);\\r\\n  expect(screen.getByText(\'Card 1 of 20\')).toBeVisible();\\r\\n  expect(screen.queryByRole(\'textbox\')).toBeNull();\\r\\n  const card = answer(true);\\r\\n  expect(document.querySelector(\'.iw-stage\')).toHaveAttribute(\'data-result\', \'right\');\\r\\n  expect(document.querySelector(\'.iw-back\')!.textContent).toContain(`Group ${card.group}`);\\r\\n  expect(screen.getByText(\'Correct\')).toBeInTheDocument();\\r\\n});\\r\\n\\r\\nit(\'shakes and corrects on a wrong choice, then brings the card back once at the end of the round\', () => {\\r\\n  render(<IndicatorWordDeck/>);\\r\\n  const missed = answer(false);\\r\\n  expect(document.querySelector(\'.iw-stage\')).toHaveAttribute(\'data-result\', \'wrong\');\\r\\n  expect(screen.getByText(\'Not quite\')).toBeInTheDocument();\\r\\n  expect(document.querySelector(\'.iw-back\')!.textContent).toContain(`Group ${missed.group}`);\\r\\n  click(\'Next card\');\\r\\n  for (let i = 1; i < 20; i += 1) { answer(true); click(i === 19 ? \'Next card\' : \'Next card\'); }\\r\\n  expect(screen.getByText(\'Second look 1 of 1\')).toBeVisible();\\r\\n  expect(displayWord(shownCard().word)).toBe(displayWord(missed.word));\\r\\n  answer(true);\\r\\n  click(\'See my results\');\\r\\n  expect(screen.getByRole(\'heading\', { name: \'19 right first time.\' })).toBeVisible();\\r\\n  expect(screen.getByText(/1 needed a second look/)).toBeVisible();\\r\\n  click(\'Go again\');\\r\\n  expect(screen.getByText(\'Card 1 of 20\')).toBeVisible();\\r\\n});\\r\\n\\r\\nit(\'resumes on the same card, mid-answer, after the page is reopened\', () => {\\r\\n  const view = render(<IndicatorWordDeck/>);\\r\\n  answer(true); click(\'Next card\');\\r\\n  answer(false);\\r\\n  const word = document.querySelector(\'.iw-front .iw-word\')!.textContent;\\r\\n  view.unmount();\\r\\n  render(<IndicatorWordDeck/>);\\r\\n  expect(screen.getByText(\'Card 2 of 20\')).toBeVisible();\\r\\n  expect(document.querySelector(\'.iw-front .iw-word\')!.textContent).toBe(word);\\r\\n  expect(screen.getByRole(\'button\', { name: \'Next card\' })).toBeVisible();\\r\\n  expect(document.querySelector(\'.iw-stage\')).toHaveAttribute(\'data-result\', \'wrong\');\\r\\n});\\r\\n\\r\\nit(\'saves every answer to the course record, and resumes from it when the device copy is gone\', () => {\\r\\n  let store: ReturnType<typeof useDeviceCourseProgress> | undefined;\\r\\n  function Provider() { store = useDeviceCourseProgress(\'deck-test\'); return <CourseProgressProvider store={store}><IndicatorWordDeck/></CourseProgressProvider>; }\\r\\n  const view = render(<Provider/>);\\r\\n  answer(true); click(\'Next card\'); answer(true);\\r\\n  const saved = store!.steps[\'flashcards-indicators\'];\\r\\n  expect(saved.state).toBeTruthy();\\r\\n  expect(saved).toMatchObject({ status: \'started\', done: 2, total: 20 });\\r\\n  view.unmount();\\r\\n  localStorage.removeItem(\'conditional-indicator-words-v1\');\\r\\n  render(<Provider/>);\\r\\n  expect(screen.getByText(\'Card 2 of 20\')).toBeVisible();\\r\\n  expect(screen.getByRole(\'button\', { name: \'Next card\' })).toBeVisible();\\r\\n});\\r\\n"}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"121744","wall_time_seconds":0.4086046,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":619,"output":"import { useId, useState } from \'react\';\\r\\nimport { sufficientConditionFilms } from \'../content/sufficientConditionFilms\';\\r\\n\\r\\nexport type SufficientConditionFilmAsset = { id: string; title: string; src: string; poster?: string; captions: string; caption: string; transcript: string };\\r\\n\\r\\n/** Render only an actual generated asset; the written lesson remains available without video. */\\r\\nexport function SufficientConditionFilm({ assets = sufficientConditionFilms, title = \'What the rule gives us\', ariaLabel = \'Short explanations of sufficiency\' }: { assets?: SufficientConditionFilmAsset[]; title?: string; ariaLabel?: string }) {\\r\\n  const [selected, setSelected] = useState(0);\\r\\n  const [failedId, setFailedId] = useState<string | null>(null);\\r\\n  const [attempt, setAttempt] = useState(0);\\r\\n  const videoId = useId();\\r\\n  const asset = assets[selected];\\r\\n  if (!asset) return null;\\r\\n  const failed = failedId === asset.id;\\r\\n  return <section className=\\"sc-film\\" aria-label={ariaLabel}>\\r\\n    <header><p className=\\"sc-eyebrow\\">Watch the explanation</p><h3>{title}</h3></header>\\r\\n    {assets.length > 1 && <div className=\\"sc-film-selector\\" role=\\"group\\" aria-label=\\"Choose an explanation\\">\\r\\n      {assets.map((clip, index) => <button type=\\"button\\" key={clip.id}\\r\\n        aria-pressed={index === selected} aria-controls={videoId}\\r\\n        onClick={() => { setSelected(index); setFailedId(null); }}>{clip.title}</button>)}\\r\\n    </div>}\\r\\n    <figure>\\r\\n      <video id={videoId} key={`${asset.id}-${attempt}`} controls preload=\\"metadata\\" playsInline\\r\\n        poster={asset.poster} aria-label={asset.caption} onError={() => setFailedId(asset.id)}>\\r\\n        <source src={asset.src} type=\\"video/mp4\\" onError={() => setFailedId(asset.id)}/>\\r\\n        <track kind=\\"captions\\" src={asset.captions} srcLang=\\"en\\" label=\\"English\\" default/>\\r\\n        Your browser cannot play this clip. The explanation is available below.\\r\\n      </video>\\r\\n      <figcaption>{asset.caption}</figcaption>\\r\\n    </figure>\\r\\n    {failed && <div className=\\"sc-film-error\\">\\r\\n      <p role=\\"alert\\">The video couldn\'t load. You can read the explanation below or try again.</p>\\r\\n      <button type=\\"button\\" onClick={() => { setFailedId(null); setAttempt(value => value + 1); }}>Try loading the video again</button>\\r\\n    </div>}\\r\\n    <details key={`transcript-${asset.id}-${failed}`} open={failed || undefined}>\\r\\n      <summary>Read the video explanation</summary><p>{asset.transcript}</p>\\r\\n    </details>\\r\\n  </section>;\\r\\n}\\r\\n"}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"154f5a","wall_time_seconds":0.6862294,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":324,"output":"/** The indicator-word deck. One card per word, both groups mixed. Word list confirmed by Joshua 2026-10-11. */\\r\\nexport type IndicatorGroup = 1 | 2;\\r\\nexport interface IndicatorWordCard { id: string; word: string; group: IndicatorGroup }\\r\\n\\r\\nconst group1 = [\'if\', \'when\', \'whenever\', \'where\', \'all\', \'every\', \'any\', \'anyone\', \'anytime\', \'every time\', \'each\', \'the only\', \'who\'];\\r\\nconst group2 = [\'only\', \'only if\', \'only when\', \'only where\', \'always\', \'must\', \'requires\'];\\r\\nconst slug = (word: string) => word.replace(/\\\\s+/g, \'-\');\\r\\n\\r\\nexport const indicatorWordCards: IndicatorWordCard[] = [\\r\\n  ...group1.map(word => ({ id: `g1-${slug(word)}`, word, group: 1 as const })),\\r\\n  ...group2.map(word => ({ id: `g2-${slug(word)}`, word, group: 2 as const })),\\r\\n];\\r\\n\\r\\nexport const displayWord = (word: string) => word.charAt(0).toUpperCase() + word.slice(1);\\r\\nexport const groupKind = (group: IndicatorGroup) => group === 1 ? \'sufficient\' : \'necessary\';\\r\\n/** The one line on the back of every card. */\\r\\nexport const groupSentence = (group: IndicatorGroup) => `This is a ${groupKind(group)} condition indicator word, Group ${group}.`;\\r\\n/** Sufficient ideas go on the left of the arrow, necessary ideas on the right. */\\r\\nexport const groupSide = (group: IndicatorGroup) => group === 1 ? \'left\' : \'right\';\\r\\n"}'}]

## assistant

Implemented in `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os-projects\lsatu-conditional-takeover`:

- **IndicatorWordDeck:** Account sessions no longer inherit or overwrite the shared anonymous device round.
- **TermDeck:** Saves the card, revealed side, and cards reviewed to account progress. Completion requires turning all four cards.
- **FlashcardTools:** Shows only decks covered by assigned units, including blocking direct unauthorized deck links.
- Added account-isolation and resume/completion regression tests.

**Verification:** 11 tests passed across `IndicatorWordDeck.test.tsx`, `TermDeck.test.tsx`, and `FlashcardTools.test.tsx`. No browser verification performed because root owns the live browser.

Remaining findings delivered to root:

1. `NecessaryNote.tsx:88-99`: Passport obligation becomes a report of completed action in feedback. Replace the rule with a factual restriction or preserve the obligation.
2. `NecessaryNote.tsx:146-147`: The proposed counterexample does not establish necessity; clarify that the rule establishes necessity and the counterexample only disproves sufficiency.
3. `OpeningCourse.tsx:174` with `SufficientConditionFilm.tsx:23`: Hidden previously visited lessons keep video playback mounted. Pause hidden videos on navigation.
4. Quiz role colors disclose sufficient/necessary answers before the student answers.
5. Lesson quizzes and examples lose interaction state on reload; root is fixing their persistence and completion.

The approved 20 indicator words and their grouping remain unchanged. All 12 drill acceptance paths and a shared wrong-answer/resume/completion checklist have been sent to root.

(next as (prior: T) => T)(current.current) : next;\\r\\n    current.current = result;\\r\\n    setValue(result);\\r\\n    if (stepId) store?.save(stepId, { state: { lessonInteractions: { [key]: result } } });\\r\\n  }, [key, stepId, store?.save]);\\r\\n  return [value, change];\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nexport function CourseProgressProvider({ store, children }: { store: CourseProgressStore; children: ReactNode }) {\\r\\n  return <CourseProgressContext.Provider value={store}>{children}</CourseProgressContext.Provider>;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nexport function useCourseProgress() {\\r\\n  return useContext(CourseProgressContext);\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * One lesson or drill\'s saved progress. Returns null outside a course (isolated\\r\\n * component tests), where drills fall back to their own device storage.\\r\\n */\\r\\nexport function useCourseStep(stepId: string | undefined) {\\r\\n  const store = useContext(CourseProgressContext);\\r\\n  const save = store?.save;\\r\\n  const record = useCallback((patch: CourseStepPatch) => { if (stepId) save?.(stepId, patch); }, [save, stepId]);\\r\\n  if (!store || !stepId) return null;\\r\\n  return { ready: store.ready, saved: store.steps[stepId], record };\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/** Device-only store for the preview harness and signed-out use; the course route uses the account. */\\r\\nexport function useDeviceCourseProgress(scope = \'preview\'): CourseProgressStore {\\r\\n  const key = `conditional-course-progress:${scope}`;\\r\\n  const read = () => {\\r\\n    try { return JSON.parse(localStorage.getItem(key) ?? \'{}\') ?? {}; } catch { return {}; }\\r\\n  };\\r\\n  const [snapshot, setSnapshot] = useState<{ key: string; steps: Record<string, CourseStepProgress> }>(() => ({ key, steps: read() }));\\r\\n  useEffect(() => { if (snapshot.key !== key) setSnapshot({ key, steps: read() }); }, [key, snapshot.key]);\\r\\n  const save = useCallback((stepId: string, patch: CourseStepPatch) => setSnapshot(snapshot => {\\r\\n    const previous = snapshot.key === key ? snapshot.steps : {};\\r\\n    const prior = previous[stepId];\\r\\n    const status = prior?.status === \'done\' ? \'done\' : patch.status ?? prior?.status ?? \'started\';\\r\\n    const next = { ...previous, [stepId]: { ...prior, ...patch, ...(patch.state !== undefined ? { state: mergeCourseStepState(prior?.state, patch.state) } : {}), status, updatedAt: new Date().toISOString() } };\\r\\n    try { localStorage.setItem(key, JSON.stringify(next)); } catch { /* keep in memory */ }\\r\\n    return { key, steps: next };\\r\\n  }), [key]);\\r\\n  return useMemo(() => ({ ready: snapshot.key === key, steps: snapshot.key === key ? It is the sufficient condition, so it goes on the left.\',\\r\\n      \'Being a student is the necessary condition in this rule. It belongs on the right, because enrollment requires it.\',\\r\\n      \'If points to the sufficient condition, but it is not itself a condition. Keep the whole idea it introduces: you are enrolled in law school.\',\\r\\n    ],\\r\\n    explanation: \'Read from the sufficient condition toward the necessary condition: You are enrolled in law school → You are a student.\',\\r\\n    correct: 0,\\r\\n  },\\r\\n  {\\r\\n    id: \'arrow-right\',\\r\\n    rule: \'If you are enrolled in law school, then you are a student.\',\\r\\n    prompt: \'Which complete condition belongs on the right side of the arrow?\',\\r\\n    options: [\'You are enrolled in law school.\', \'You are a student.\', \'Law school.\'],\\r\\n    feedback: [\\r\\n      \'Enrollment is the sufficient condition. Enrollment on the left requires being a student on the right.\',\\r\\n    correct: 1,\\r\\n  },\\r\\n  {\\r\\n    id: \'arrow-reading\',\\r\\n    rule: \'This folder is marked complete → This folder contains a signed form\',\\r\\n    prompt: \'How do you read this arrow?\',\\r\\n    options: [\\r\\n      \'If this folder contains a signed form, it must be marked complete.\',\\r\\n      \'This folder is marked complete and contains a signed form.\',\\r\\n      \'If this folder is marked complete, it must contain a signed form.\',\\r\\n    ],\\r\\n    feedback: [\\r\\n      \'That reads the arrow backward. Start with this folder is marked complete on the left, then follow the arrow to the signed form on the right.\',\\r\\n      \'That treats both sides as facts. The arrow tells us what must follow if the left side is true; it does not say either side is actually true.\',\\r\\n      \'That follows the arrow from left to right and keeps the requirement: if this folder is marked complete, the signed form must be there.\',\\r\\n    ],\\r\\n    explanation: \'Read the arrow as must: if the left side is true, the right side must be true.\',\\r\\n    correct: 2,\\r\\n  },\\r\\n  {\\r\\n    id: \'arrow-clause-order\',\\r\\n    rule: \'The library extends your loan if you renew online.\',\\r\\n    prompt: \'Which arrow keeps the meaning of this rule?\',\\r\\n    options: [\\r\\n      \'The library extends your loan → You renew online\',\\r\\n      \'You renew online → The library extends your loan\',\\r\\n      \'If → The library extends your loan\',\\r\\n    ],\\r\\n    feedback: [\\r\\n      \'That follows the rule order but reverses the requirement. Renewing online is enough to prove that the library extends your loan, even though renewing appears last.\',\\r\\n      \'Renewing online is the sufficient condition. If introduces it at the end of this rule, so it still belongs on the left of the arrow.\',\\r\\n      \'If is an indicator, not a complete condition. Put the idea it introduces on the left: you renew online.\',\\r\\n    ],\\r\\n    explanation: \'This has the same meaning as If you renew online, the library extends your loan. Moving the words does not change which condition is sufficient or necessary.\',\\r\\n    correct: 1,\\r\\n  },\\r\\n];\\r\\n\\r\\nexport function MustArrowPractice() {\\r\\n  const [index, setIndex] = useState(0);\\r\\n  const [answers, setAnswers] = useState<Record<string, number>>({});\\r\\n  const [choice, setChoice] = useState<number | null>(null);\\r\\n  const heading = useRef<HTMLHeadingElement>(null);\\r\\n  const feedbackHeading = useRef<HTMLHeadingElement>(null);\\r\\n  const focusFeedback = useRef(false);\\r\\n  const id = useId();\\r\\n  const question = questions[index];\\r\\n  const answer = answers[question.id];\\r\\n  const answered = answer !== undefined;\\r\\n  const count = Object.keys(answers).length;\\r\\n\\r\\n  useEffect(() => {\\r\\n    if (focusFeedback.current) {\\r\\n      feedbackHeading.current?.focus();\\r\\n      focusFeedback.current = false;\\r\\n    }\\r\\n  }, [answers]);\\r\\n\\r\\n  function move(next: number) {\\r\\n    setIndex(next);\\r\\n    setChoice(null);\\r\\n    heading.current?.focus();\\r\\n  }\\r\\n\\r\\n  return <div className=\\"ma-question-practice\\">\\r\\n    <div className=\\"ma-question-practice-progress\\">\\r\\n      <span id={`${id}-progress`}>{count} answered · {questions.length - count} remaining</span>\\r\\n      <progress aria-labelledby={`${id}-progress`} value={count} max={questions.length}/>\\r\\n    </div>\\r\\n    <div className=\\"ma-question-practice-question\\">\\r\\n      <h4 ref={heading} tabIndex={-1}>Question {index + 1} of {questions.length}</h4>\\r\\n      <p className=\\"ma-question-practice-rule\\">{question.rule}</p>\\r\\n      <fieldset className=\\"ma-question-practice-choices\\" disabled={answered}>\\r\\n        <legend className=\\"ma-question-practice-prompt\\">{question.prompt}</legend>\\r\\n        {question.options.map((option, optionIndex) => <label key={option} className={(answered ? answer : choice) === optionIndex ? \'is-selected\' : undefined}>\\r\\n          <input type=\\"radio\\" name={`${id}-${question.id}`} checked={(answered ? answer : choice) === optionIndex}\\r\\n            onChange={() => setChoice(optionIndex)}/>\\r\\n          <span>{option}</span>\\r\\n        </label>)}\\r\\n      </fieldset>\\r\\n      {!answered && <button type=\\"button\\" disabled={choice === null} onClick={() => {\\r\\n        if (choice !== null) {\\r\\n          focusFeedback.current = true;\\r\\n          setAnswers(items => ({ ...items, [question.id]: choice }));\\r\\n        }\\r\\n      }}>Check answer</button>}\\r\\n      <div className=\\"ma-question-practice-feedback\\">\\r\\n        {answered && <div className=\\"ma-question-practice-explanation\\">\\r\\n          <h5 ref={feedbackHeading} tabIndex={-1}>{answer === question.correct ? \'Correct.\' : \'Not quite.\'}</h5>\\r\\n          <p>{question.feedback[answer]}</p>\\r\\n          {answer !== question.correct && <p><strong>The answer: {question.options[question.correct]}</strong></p>}\\r\\n          <p>{question.explanation}</p>\\r\\n          {count === questions.length && <p className=\\"ma-question-practice-finished\\">You\'ve answered all {questions.length} questions. Review the explanations, then continue to the next drill.</p>}\\r\\n        </div>}\\r\\n      </div>\\r\\n    </div>\\r\\n    <div className=\\"ma-question-practice-navigation\\">\\r\\n      <button type=\\"button\\" disabled={index === 0} onClick={() => move(index - 1)}>Previous question</button>\\r\\n      {index < questions.length - 1 && <button type=\\"button\\" disabled={!answered} onClick={() => move(index + 1)}>Next question <span aria-hidden=\\"true\\">→</span></button>}\\r\\n    </div>\\r\\n  </div>;\\r\\n}\\r\\n"}'}]

\'Hide the reasoning\' : \'Show the reasoning\'}</button><div id={answerId} aria-live=\\"polite\\">{revealed && <div className=\\"co-pause-answer\\">{children}</div>}</div></div>;\\r\\n}\\r\\nfunction Check({ prompt, options, correct, feedback }: { prompt: string; options: string[]; correct: number; feedback: string[] }) {\\r\\n  const [choice, setChoice] = useState<number | null>(null);\\r\\n  if (!Array.isArray(feedback) || feedback.length !== options.length || feedback.some(message => typeof message !== \'string\' || !message.trim())) {\\r\\n    throw new Error(`Every answer choice needs an explanation: ${prompt}`);\\r\\n  }\\r\\n  return <div className=\\"co-check\\"><p className=\\"co-kicker\\">Try it</p><h3>{prompt}</h3><div className=\\"co-choices\\">{options.map((option, i) => <button key={option} type=\\"button\\" aria-pressed={choice === i} onClick={() => setChoice(i)}>{option}</button>)}</div><div role=\\"status\\">{choice !== null && <div className={`co-feedback ${choice === correct ? \'co-correct\' : \'\'}`} ><span className=\\"co-answer-state\\">{choice === correct ? \'Correct.\' : \'Not quite.\'}</span><strong>Your choice: {options[choice]}</strong><p>{feedback[choice]}</p></div>}</div></div>;\\r\\n}\\r\\nfunction GateExplorer() {\\r\\n  const [fact, setFact] = useState<\'win\' | \'bicycle\'>(\'win\');\\r\\n  return <div className=\\"co-gate\\"><p className=\\"co-kicker\\">Enough and required</p><Arrow left=\\"Win the raffle\\" right=\\"Get a bicycle\\"/><div className=\\"co-choices\\"><button aria-pressed={fact === \'win\'} onClick={() => setFact(\'win\')}>You win the raffle</button><button aria-pressed={fact === \'bicycle\'} onClick={() => setFact(\'bicycle\')}>You get a bicycle</button></div><div className=\\"co-gate-track\\"><div className={fact === \'win\' ? \'co-lit\' : \'\'}>Win the raffle<span>Sufficient: enough</span></div><b aria-hidden=\\"true\\">→</b><span className=\\"co-sr-only\\">implies</span><div className=\\"co-lit\\">Get a bicycle<span>Necessary: required</span></div></div><p role=\\"status\\">{fact === \'win\' ? \'You get a bicycle because the rule gives you that result if you win.\' : \'You get a bicycle. Winning remains unknown: you could have bought it or received it as a gift instead.\'}</p></div>;\\r\\n}\\r\\ntype Translation = { word: string; sentence: string; target: string; other: string; left: string; right: string; why: string; readback: string };\\r\\ntype MappedTranslation = Translation & { sentenceTarget: string };\\r\\ntype DiagramTranslation = MappedTranslation & { reversedPromise: string };\\r\\n\\r\\nexport function orderedConditionalIdeas(item: Pick<Translation, \'sentence\' | \'target\' | \'other\'>): [string, string] {\\r\\n  let hash = 2166136261;\\r\\n  for (const character of item.sentence) hash = Math.imul(hash ^ character.charCodeAt(0), 16777619);\\r\\n  const reverseOrder = ((hash ^ (hash >>> 16)) >>> 0) % 2 === 1;\\r\\n  return reverseOrder ? [item.other, item.target] : [item.target, item.other];\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nexport const translationExamples: Record<string, MappedTranslation[]> = {\\r\\n  \'group-1\': [\\r\\n    { word: \'if\', sentence: \'A report is reviewed if it is submitted before noon.\', sentenceTarget: \'it is submitted before noon\', target: \'Submitted before noon\', other: \'Reviewed\', left: \'Submitted before noon\', right: \'Reviewed\', why: \'If what? Submission before noon. The sufficient clause comes second, but it still goes left.\', readback: \'If a report is submitted before noon, then it is reviewed.\' },\\r\\n    { word: \'when\', sentence: \'When the alarm sounds, the staff evacuate.\', sentenceTarget: \'the alarm sounds\', target: \'Alarm sounds\', other: \'Staff evacuate\', left: \'Alarm sounds\', right: \'Staff evacuate\', why: \'When what? The alarm sounds. The rule says every alarm brings an evacuation.\', readback: \'If the alarm sounds, then the staff evacuate.\' },\\r\\n    { word: \'where\', sentence: \'Where a station is available, the service operates.\', sentenceTarget: \'a station is available\', target: \'Station available\', other: \'Service operates\', left: \'Station available\', right: \'Service operates\', why: \'Where what? A station is available. This rule makes station availability enough for service.\', readback: \'If a station is available at a location, then the service operates there.\' },\\r\\n    { word: \'all\', sentence: \'All registered guests receive a wristband.\', sentenceTarget: \'registered guests\', target: \'Registered guest\', other: \'Receives wristband\', left: \'Registered guest\', right: \'Receives wristband\', why: \'All what? Registered guests. Keep registered; guest alone does not meet the whole condition.\', readback: \'If someone is a registered guest, then that person receives a wristband.\' },\\r\\n    { word: \'the only\', sentence: \'The only vehicles permitted inside are electric vehicles.\', sentenceTarget: \'vehicles permitted inside\', target: \'Vehicle permitted inside\', other: \'Electric vehicle\', left: \'Vehicle permitted inside\', right: \'Electric vehicle\', why: \'The only what? Vehicles permitted inside. Every permitted vehicle must be electric. Finalist. That meets the requirement without proving the result. Booking \\\\u2192 Receives key would promise a key to every booked guest.\\"\\r\\n    },\\r\\n    {\\r\\n      \\"word\\": \\"only if\\",\\r\\n      \\"sentence\\": \\"A printer releases a document only if its job has been approved.\\",\\r\\n      \\"sentenceTarget\\": \\"its job has been approved\\",\\r\\n      \\"target\\": \\"Job approved\\",\\r\\n      \\"other\\": \\"Printer releases document\\",\\r\\n      \\"left\\": \\"Printer releases document\\",\\r\\n      \\"right\\": \\"Job approved\\",\\r\\n      \\"readback\\": \\"If a printer releases a document, then its job has been approved.\\",\\r\\n      \\"why\\": \\"The printer needs an approved job to release the document. The rule requires approval for release; it doesn\'t guarantee release for every approved job. Your arrow would make approval enough.\\"\\r\\n    },\\r\\n    {\\r\\n      \\"word\\": \\"only when\\",\\r\\n      \\"sentence\\": \\"The fountain runs only when its pump is switched on.\\",\\r\\n      \\"sentenceTarget\\": \\"its pump is switched on\\",\\r\\n      \\"target\\": \\"Pump switched on\\",\\r\\n      \\"other\\": \\"Fountain runs\\",\\r\\n      \\"left\\": \\"Fountain runs\\",\\r\\n      \\"right\\": \\"Pump switched on\\",\\r\\n      \\"readback\\": \\"If the fountain runs, then its pump is switched on.\\",\\r\\n      \\"why\\": \\"If the fountain is running, its pump is switched on. That\'s what only when requires. The pump could be on while the fountain isn\'t running; nothing in the rule rules that out.\\",\\r\\n      \\"reversedPromise\\": \\"The pump could be on while the fountain isn\'t running. That fits the stated requirement. Your arrow would exclude it by turning the pump\'s switched-on state into a guarantee of running.\\"\\r\\n    },\\r\\n    {\\r\\n      \\"word\\": \\"only where\\",\\r\\n      \\"sentence\\": \\"A delivery van stops only where parking is permitted.\\",\\r\\n      \\"senten…12012 tokens truncated…ns leaves room for exceptions; a conditional rule makes its consequence certain whenever its sufficient condition holds.</p>\\r\\n      <p>Read the arrow as <strong>must</strong>. The sufficient condition goes on the left. The necessary condition goes on the right. If the left condition is true, the right condition must be true too. The arrow doesn\'t say which event happened first, and it doesn\'t prove that one event caused the other.</p>\\r\\n    </Section>\\r\\n    <Section title={first ? \'Different wording can make the same rule\' : \'Read it as a requirement\'}>\\r\\n      {first ? <>\\r\\n        <p>Suppose a studio has this rule: every painting with a silver seal has been inspected. Having the seal is enough to prove inspection. Let\'s keep that meaning and change the wording.</p>\\r\\n        <div className=\\"co-word-comparison\\"><div><blockquote>If a painting has a silver seal, it has been inspected.</blockquote><p>If what? It has a silver seal. That complete idea goes on the left.</p></div><div><blockquote>A painting has been inspected whenever it has a silver seal.</blockquote><p>Whenever what? It has a silver seal. The sufficient condition comes later in this rule, but it still goes on the left.</p></div></div>\\r\\n        <Arrow left=\\"Has silver seal\\" right=\\"Has been inspected\\"/>\\r\\n        <p>The indicator can appear at the beginning or in the middle. Sentence position doesn\'t choose the side of the arrow. Find the idea the indicator refers to, then ask what the rule tells you if that idea is true.</p>\\r\\n        <ThinkPause prompt=\\"Each painting with a silver seal has been inspected. Did changing every to each change the rule?\\">\\r\\n          <p>No. Each still covers every painting with that seal. A painting with the seal that hadn\'t been inspected would break either statement. Keep has silver seal on the left and has been inspected on the right.</p>\\r\\n        </ThinkPause>\\r\\n        <p>Some wording presents a rule: <strong>if, when, whenever</strong>. Some covers a whole group: <strong>all, each, any, every</strong>. Read each in its sentence. Any in “Do you have any paint?” asks a question; it doesn\'t give us a rule.</p>\\r\\n        <p>Other sentences promise an outcome: “A silver seal ensures the painting has been inspected” or “A silver seal implies the painting has been inspected.” Here, the promised outcome is inspection. Ensures and implies express the arrow between the two complete ideas. Leads to, will, and always can also express a definite result in a rule, but the word alone doesn\'t settle the meaning. “The studio will open tomorrow” gives us no sufficient condition to put on the left.</p>\\r\\n        <p>Try the next sentences by imagining the condition each one covers. If you know a guest is registered, what follows? If you know a report arrived before noon, what follows? Each indicator will help us find that starting condition.</p>\\r\\n      </> : <>\\r\\n        <p>A useful way to read the whole rule is: <strong>the left condition requires the right condition</strong>. Suppose borrowing a studio camera requires a current membership. What is required for borrowing? A current membership. That\'s the necessary condition.</p>\\r\\n        <div className=\\"co-word-comparison\\"><div><blockquote>Borrowing a studio camera requires a current membership.</blockquote><p>Requires names what borrowing needs: a current membership. An expired membership would not meet that requirement.</p></div><div><blockquote>A current membership is required to borrow a studio camera.</blockquote><p>The same required idea now comes first. Moving it in the rule hasn\'t changed what borrowing requires.</p></div></div>\\r\\n        <Arrow left=\\"Borrows studio camera\\" right=\\"Has current membership\\"/>\\r\\n        <ThinkPause prompt=\\"Mina has a current membership. Does this rule tell us she borrowed a camera?\\">\\r\\n          <p>No. Membership is something borrowing requires. Mina could have the membership and never borrow a camera. The rule tells us what we need if she borrows; having what she needs doesn\'t tell us she did.</p>\\r\\n        </ThinkPause>\\r\\n        <p>Notice the meaning shared by <strong>requires, necessitates, depends on,</strong> and <strong>relies on</strong> when they express a strict requirement. If borrowing depends on having a current membership, borrowing still requires membership. “Depends partly on” may describe an influence instead, so read the whole claim before treating it as a conditional.</p>\\r\\n        <p>You may also hear the requirement as “In order to borrow a camera, you must have a current membership.” The goal is borrowing; the required condition is membership. “Only people with a current membership borrow a camera” and “You borrow a camera only if you have a current membership” keep membership required. Neither tells us that every member borrows one.</p>\\r\\n        <Check prompt=\\"Using the recording booth depends on having a reservation. What belongs on the right?\\" options={[\'Uses recording booth\', \'Has a reservation\']} correct={1} feedback={[\'Using the booth is what needs the reservation. Putting use on the right would make a reservation enough to prove that someone used the booth, which the rule never says.\', \'A reservation is required for using the booth, so it belongs on the right. Having a reservation still leaves open whether the person uses it.\']}/>\\r\\n        <p>You won\'t always see a familiar word. Find what the rule says is required <strong>for the other condition</strong>. Then check that your arrow keeps that requirement on the right.</p>\\r\\n      </>}\\r\\n    </Section>\\r\\n    {IndicatorSections({ id })}\\r\\n    <Section id=\\"condition-practice\\" title={first ? \'Which reports does this rule cover?\' : \'What kind of pass does entry require?\'}>\\r\\n      {first ? <>\\r\\n        <blockquote>A report is reviewed if it is submitted before noon.</blockquote>\\r\\n        <p>If what? If the report is submitted before noon. Keep before noon with submitted. A report arriving at four in the afternoon does not meet the condition this rule gives us.</p>\\r\\n        <p>Now suppose the report does arrive before noon. We know it is reviewed. Submission before noon is enough to prove review, so that condition belongs on the left of our arrow.</p>\\r\\n      </> : <>\\r\\n        <blockquote>Entry requires a current pass.</blockquote>\\r\\n        <p>What does entry require? A current pass. Current matters: an expired pass would not meet the requirement. Requires refers to that complete requirement, even if we rewrite the rule as “A current pass is required for entry.”</p>\\r\\n        <p>Suppose someone enters. Under this rule, that person has a current pass. That puts entry on the left and the required pass on the right. Someone with a current pass could still stay outside.</p>\\r\\n      </>}\\r\\n      <p>This is the <strong>What Test</strong>: ask what the indicator refers to, and answer with the words that make the condition complete. All what? Registered guests. Only if what? A parent signs the consent form. Guest alone drops registration; a parent alone drops the signature.</p>\\r\\n      <p>Try that question on each rule below. When your answer includes the complete condition, the rule will highlight its words. Then show the diagram and ask what you would know if its left condition were true. The right condition should be exactly what the rule lets you conclude.</p>\\r\\n      <TranslationLab id={id}/>\\r\\n    </Section>\\r\\n    <Section title=\\"Keep the whole condition\\" dark>\\r\\n      <blockquote>{first ? \'Any applicant who submits late pays a fee.\' : \'Entry requires a current pass.\'}</blockquote>\\r\\n      <p>{first\\r\\n        ? \\"Applicant alone isn\'t the condition. The rule is about an applicant who submits late. Keep that restriction when you shorten the idea; otherwise your diagram would make a claim about on-time applicants too.\\"\\r\\n        : \\"A current pass is required. Has a pass drops current and would include an expired pass. Shortening a label should save writing while keeping what the rule requires.\\"}</p>\\r\\n      <Check prompt={first ? \'What goes left in this rule?\' : \'What goes right in this rule?\'} options={first ? [\'Applicant\', \'Applicant submits late\'] : [\'Has current pass\', \'Has any pass\']} correct={first ? 1 : 0} feedback={first\\r\\n        ? [\'You dropped the late-submission restriction. Applicant alone would cover every applicant, including someone who submits on time. The sufficient idea is applicant submits late.\', \'This keeps the complete sufficient idea. The rule covers applicants who submit late, so keep that restriction with the applicant.\']\\r\\n        : [\'This keeps the full requirement. Entry needs a current pass; an expired pass does not meet that requirement.\', \'You dropped current from the requirement. Has any pass would include an expired pass, which is not what the rule requires.\']}/>\\r\\n    </Section>\\r\\n    <Section title={first ? \'Read the only as a complete phrase\' : \'Compare only with the only\'}>\\r\\n      {first ? <>\\r\\n        <blockquote>The only people admitted are members.</blockquote>\\r\\n        <p>The only what? The only people admitted. The complete group is admitted people. If someone is admitted, the rule tells us they\'re a member.</p>\\r\\n        <Arrow left=\\"Admitted\\" right=\\"Member\\"/>\\r\\n        <p>A member who stays outside fits this rule. Nothing says every member is admitted. Check that your diagram keeps that possibility open.</p>\\r\\n        <Check prompt=\\"The only notebooks kept on this shelf are hardback. Which diagram keeps the rule?\\" options={[\'Hardback → Kept on shelf\', \'Kept on shelf → Hardback\']} correct={1} feedback={[\'You made being hardback enough for a place on the shelf. The rule limits the notebooks kept there; it does not put every hardback notebook on this shelf.\', \'A notebook kept on this shelf has to be hardback. A hardback notebook on another shelf would also fit the rule, so this direction keeps what the rule actually says.\']}/>\\r\\n      </> : <>\\r\\n        <p>Try the distinction on the raffle rule we already understand. The wording changes, but winning still tells us that you get a bicycle.</p>\\r\\n        <div className=\\"co-word-comparison\\"><div><blockquote>Only people who get a bicycle win this raffle.</blockquote><p>Only refers to people who get a bicycle. Getting one is required whenever someone wins, so get a bicycle goes right.</p></div><div><blockquote>The only people who win this raffle are people who get a bicycle.</blockquote><p>The only refers to people who win. Winning is enough to prove getting a bicycle, so win the raffle goes left.</p></div></div>\\r\\n        <Arrow left=\\"Win the raffle\\" right=\\"Get a bicycle\\"/>\\r\\n        <p>Both sentences keep the same direction. Someone who gets a bicycle as a gift without winning fits both. Neither sentence makes getting a bicycle enough to prove winning.</p>\\r\\n        <Check prompt=\\"Only registered guests receive wristbands. What follows from receiving a wristband?\\" options={[\'The person is a registered guest.\', \'Nothing about registration.\']} correct={0} feedback={[\'Only limits wristbands to registered guests, so receiving one proves that the person is registered.\', \'You treated the rule like all registered guests receive wristbands. Only restricts who receives one, so the wristband does prove that the person is a registered guest.\']}/>\\r\\n      </>}\\r\\n    </Section>\\r\\n    <Section title=\\"From sentence to diagram\\">\\r\\n      <p>You have seen the indicators with help. Now use a new sentence. Identify both complete ideas, choose the sufficient one for the left, and check whether your diagram says what the rule says.</p>\\r\\n      <p>If you know the meaning but hesitate over the arrow, imagine the left condition is true. Does the rule then require the right condition? Read the whole rule in English. If it makes a promise the original didn\'t make, change the direction.</p>\\r\\n      <DiagramBuilder id={id}/>\\r\\n      <details className=\\"co-practical-note\\"><summary>Why write a diagram when I understand the rule?</summary><p>For one short sentence, you may be able to hold the connection in your head. Here, writing it makes your direction and complete conditions visible so you can check them. If your first diagram doesn\'t match the meaning, find the exact phrase you misread and revise that part.</p></details>\\r\\n    </Section>\\r\\n    <Section title=\\"Say the rule without the clues\\">\\r\\n      <p>Read the new sentence and explain its direction before revealing. If you hesitate, ask what the indicator refers to, then try the proposed rule in ordinary English. Compare your explanation with the one that appears.</p>\\r\\n      <Flashcards id={id}/>\\r\\n    </Section>\\r\\n    <Section title=\\"Check the meaning, not just the word\\">\\r\\n      <Check prompt={first ? \'A report is reviewed if submitted before noon. Which is the same rule?\' : \'A student graduates only if the project is completed. Which is the same rule?\'} options={first ? [\'If reviewed, it was submitted before noon.\', \'If submitted before noon, it is reviewed.\'] : [\'If the student graduates, the project was completed.\', \'If the project is completed, the student graduates.\']} correct={first ? 1 : 0} feedback={first\\r\\n        ? [\'You reversed the if rule. Submission before noon is enough for review; the rule has not said review proves when the report was submitted.\', \'This keeps the if-clause as the sufficient condition. Submission before noon gives us review even though it appears second in the original sentence.\']\\r\\n        : [\'This keeps project completion as a requirement for graduation. It does not turn completing the project into a promise of graduation.\', \'You turned the only-if requirement into a guarantee. Graduation requires completion, but the rule allows a completed project without graduation.\']}/>\\r\\n      <p>The guided practice asks you to write your reasoning with the method beside you. Then the attached drill gives you fresh questions. Use the explanation to inspect the direction and complete conditions, not only whether you chose the same answer.</p>\\r\\n    </Section>\\r\\n  </>;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction OpeningFigure({ unitId }: { unitId: string }) {\\r\\n  if (unitId === \'orientation\') return <figure className=\\"co-hero-figure co-meaning-figure\\">\\r\\n    <p className=\\"co-kicker\\">What follows if a condition is true</p>\\r\\n    <p className=\\"co-hero-sentence\\">What would we know <strong>if</strong> this were true?</p>\\r\\n    <div className=\\"co-hypothetical-tag\\">A situation to imagine</div>\\r\\n    <figcaption>A conditional tells us what must be true if the first condition is true. It doesn\'t tell us that the first condition actually is true.</figcaption>\\r\\n  </figure>;\\r\\n  if (unitId === \'sufficient-necessary\') return <figure className=\\"co-hero-figure co-roles-figure\\">\\r\\n    <p className=\\"co-kicker\\">Two views of one rule</p>\\r\\n    <p className=\\"co-hero-rule\\">If you win the raffle, you get a bicycle.</p>\\r\\n    <div className=\\"co-role-compass\\"><div><span>Enough</span><strong>Win the raffle</strong><small>Proves getting a bicycle</small></div><div><span>Required</span><strong>Get a bicycle</strong><small>The result winning requires</small></div></div>\\r\\n    <figcaption>One condition is enough. The other is required by that same rule.</figcaption>\\r\\n  </figure>;\\r\\n  if (unitId === \'group-1\') return <figure className=\\"co-hero-figure co-sufficient-figure\\">\\r\\n    <p className=\\"co-kicker\\">Read the complete idea</p>\\r\\n    <p className=\\"co-hero-sentence\\">A report is reviewed <mark>if</mark> <strong>it is submitted before noon.</strong></p>\\r\\n    <div className=\\"co-hero-target\\"><span>What is enough for review?</span><strong>Submission before noon</strong></div>\\r\\n    <figcaption>The sufficient condition can come last in the rule.</figcaption>\\r\\n  </figure>;\\r\\n  return <figure className=\\"co-hero-figure co-required-figure\\">\\r\\n    <p className=\\"co-kicker\\">Read what the rule needs</p>\\r\\n    <p className=\\"co-hero-sentence\\">Entry requires a <strong>current pass.</strong></p>\\r\\n    <div className=\\"co-pass-figure\\"><span>Required for entry</span><strong>A current pass</strong><small>An expired pass won\'t meet this requirement.</small></div>\\r\\n    <figcaption>Having the pass alone doesn\'t prove entry.</figcaption>\\r\\n  </figure>;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction OpeningLessonBody({ unit, onComplete, continueHref, continueLabel = \'Continue to guided practice\', previousHref, reviewed }: ConditionalLessonNoteProps) {\\r\\n  const group = unit.id === \'group-1\' || unit.id === \'group-2\';\\r\\n  const orientation = unit.id === \'orientation\';\\r\\n  const title = group ? unit.id === \'group-1\' ? \'Group 1: what is enough?\' : \'Group 2: what is required?\' : orientation ? \'What Are Conditionals?\' : \'What is enough? What is required?\';\\r\\n  const repairTitle = orientation ? \\"Don\'t turn a rule into a case fact\\" : unit.id === \'sufficient-necessary\' ? \\"Don\'t turn a requirement into a guarantee\\" : unit.id === \'group-1\' ? \'Keep the direction and the whole condition\' : \'Keep requirements and completed actions distinct\';\\r\\n  const subtitle = orientation\\r\\n    ? \\"A conditional tells us that if one thing is true, something else has to be true too. Let\'s see what we can conclude from a rule, and what we still don\'t know.\\"\\r\\n    : group ? unit.id === \'group-1\'\\r\\n      ? \'These indicators can point to the sufficient idea. Let\'s take each one seriously before giving it a place in the diagram.\\";\\r\\n  return <div className=\\"conditional-note co-opening\\">\\r\\n    <header className={\'co-hero \' + (orientation ? \'co-intro-hero\' : \'\')}>\\r\\n      <div className=\\"co-hero-copy\\"><p className=\\"co-kicker\\">Conditional logic</p><h1>{title}</h1><p>{subtitle}</p>\\r\\n      <a href=\\"#co-reading\\">{orientation ? \'Start with the meaning\' : \'Begin reading\'} <span aria-hidden=\\"true\\">↓</span></a></div>\\r\\n      <OpeningFigure unitId={unit.id}/>\\r\\n    </header>\\r\\n    <article id=\\"co-reading\\">\\r\\n      <LessonPages pages={[\\r\\n        ...(orientation ? conditionalReferencePages() : flattenLessonPages(group ? GroupLessons({ id: unit.id }) : Fundamentals())),\\r\\n        ...(!orientation && !group ? [{ title: \'Recall the rule\', content: <Section title=\\"Recall the rule\\"><p>Leave the examples behind for a moment. [{ title: \'Return to the rule\', content: <ConditionalOpeningMedia unitId={unit.id}/> }] : []),\\r\\n        ...(orientation ? [{ title: \'Keep the rule while the words change\', content: <Section title=\\"Keep the rule while the words change\\"><RuleComparisonLab/></Section> }] : []),\\r\\n        { title: repairTitle, content: <Section id=\\"repair\\" title={repairTitle}><div className=\\"co-word-comparison\\"><div><h3>The tempting move</h3><p>{unit.commonMistake.mistake}</p></div><div><h3>Read the rule again</h3><p>{unit.commonMistake.repair}</p></div></div></Section> },\\r\\n      ]}>\\r\\n\\r\\n      <footer className=\\"co-footer\\">\\r\\n        <p>{reviewed ? \'You marked this lesson as read. [{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 6.4 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"0727e5","wall_time_seconds":0.5502683,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":2254,"output":"      \\"why\\": \\"Entering the track gives the cyclist a requirement to wear a helmet. It doesn\'t prove compliance. Keep required to wear helmet in the diagram so the stated obligation remains an obligation.\\"\\r\\n    }\\r\\n  ]\\r\\n};\\r\\nfunction TargetSentence({ item, marked }: { item: MappedTranslation; marked: boolean }) {\\r\\n  const figure = useRef<HTMLElement>(null);\\r\\n  const sentence = useRef<HTMLQuoteElement>(null);\\r\\n  const indicator = useRef<HTMLElement>(null);\\r\\n  const target = useRef<HTMLElement>(null);\\r\\n  const [connector, setConnector] = useState<string | null>(null);\\r\\n  const indicatorStart = item.sentence.toLowerCase().indexOf(item.word.toLowerCase());\\r\\n  const targetStart = item.sentence.indexOf(item.sentenceTarget);\\r\\n  if (indicatorStart < 0 || targetStart < 0) throw new Error(`Missing authored sentence span: ${item.sentence}`);\\r\\n  const spans = [\\r\\n    { start: indicatorStart, end: indicatorStart + item.word.length, kind: \'indicator\' },\\r\\n    { start: targetStart, end: targetStart + item.sentenceTarget.length, kind: \'target\' },\\r\\n  ].sort((a, b) => a.start - b.start);\\r\\n  if (spans[0].end > spans[1].start) throw new Error(`Overlapping authored sentence spans: ${item.sentence}`);\\r\\n\\r\\n  useLayoutEffect(() => {\\r\\n    if (!marked) { setConnector(null); return; }\\r\\n    const update = () => {\\r\\n      if (!figure.current || !sentence.current || !indicator.current || !target.current) return;\\r\\n      const origin = figure.current.getBoundingClientRect();\\r\\n      const firstRects = indicator.current.getClientRects();\\r\\n      const targetRects = target.current.getClientRects();\\r\\n      const first = firstRects[firstRects.length - 1];\\r\\n      const last = targetRects[targetRects.length - 1];\\r\\n      if (!first?.width || !last?.width) return;\\r\\n      const x1 = (first.left + first.right) / 2 - origin.left;\\r\\n      const x2 = (last.left + last.right) / 2 - origin.left;\\r\\n      // Project both marked spans into a dedicated rail below the whole rule.\\r\\n      // Starting under an early line could otherwise cross a later wrapped clause.\\r\\n      const railTop = sentence.current.getBoundingClientRect().bottom - origin.top + 10;\\r\\n      const baseline = railTop + 28;\\r\\n      setConnector(`M ${x1} ${railTop} V ${baseline} H ${x2} V ${railTop}`);\\r\\n    };\\r\\n    update();\\r\\n    const observer = typeof ResizeObserver === \'undefined\' ? null : new ResizeObserver(update);\\r\\n    if (figure.current) observer?.observe(figure.current);\\r\\n    window.addEventListener(\'resize\', update);\\r\\n    return () => { observer?.disconnect(); window.removeEventListener(\'resize\', update); };\\r\\n  }, [item.sentence, marked]);\\r\\n\\r\\n  if (!marked) return <blockquote>{item.sentence}</blockquote>;\\r\\n  return <figure className=\\"co-target-sentence\\" ref={figure}>\\r\\n    <blockquote ref={sentence}>{item.sentence.slice(0, spans[0].start)}\\r\\n      <mark className={`co-sentence-${spans[0].kind}`} ref={spans[0].kind === \'indicator\' ? indicator : target}>{item.sentence.slice(spans[0].start, spans[0].end)}</mark>\\r\\n      {item.sentence.slice(spans[0].end, spans[1].start)}\\r\\n      <mark className={`co-sentence-${spans[1].kind}`} ref={spans[1].kind === \'indicator\' ? indicator : target}>{item.sentence.slice(spans[1].start, spans[1].end)}</mark>\\r\\n      {item.sentence.slice(spans[1].end)}\\r\\n    </blockquote>\\r\\n    {connector && <svg className=\\"co-target-connector\\" aria-hidden=\\"true\\"><path d={connector}/></svg>}\\r\\n    <figcaption><span>Expression: <strong>{item.word}</strong></span><span>Points to: <strong>{item.target}</strong></span></figcaption>\\r\\n  </figure>;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction TranslationLab({ id }: { id: string }) {\\r\\n  const [index, setIndex] = useState(0);\\r\\n  const [choice, setChoice] = useState<string | null>(null);\\r\\n  const [diagram, setDiagram] = useState(false);\\r\\n  const item = translationExamples[id][index];\\r\\n  return <div className=\\"co-lab\\"><p className=\\"co-kicker\\">Which words does the indicator refer to?</p><div className=\\"co-word-tabs\\" role=\\"group\\" aria-label=\\"Indicator examples\\">{translationExamples[id].map((e, i) => <button key={e.word} aria-pressed={i === index} onClick={() => { setIndex(i); setChoice(null); setDiagram(false); }}>{e.word}</button>)}</div><TargetSentence item={item} marked={choice === item.target}/><p>What is <strong>{item.word}</strong> referring to?</p><div className=\\"co-choices\\">{orderedConditionalIdeas(item).map(value => <button key={value} aria-pressed={choice === value} onClick={() => { setChoice(value); setDiagram(false); }}>{value}</button>)}</div><div role=\\"status\\">{choice && <div className={`co-feedback ${choice === item.target ? \'co-correct\' : \'\'}`}><span className=\\"co-answer-state\\">{choice === item.target ? \'Correct group.\' : \'Not quite.\'}</span><strong>{choice === item.target ? \'You have the complete group.\' : \'You chose the other condition.\'}</strong>{choice !== item.target && <p>You chose {choice}, but {item.word} refers to {item.target}. {id === \'group-1\' ? \'You selected the result rather than the complete sufficient idea.\' : \'You selected the condition that needs the requirement rather than the required idea.\'}</p>}<p>{item.why}</p>{choice === item.target && <button className=\\"co-primary\\" onClick={() => setDiagram(true)}>Show the {id === \'group-1\' ? \'sufficient condition on the left\' : \'requirement on the right\'}</button>}</div>}</div>{diagram && <Arrow left={item.left} right={item.right}/>}</div>;\\r\\n}\\r\\nfunction Flashcards({ id }: { id: string }) {\\r\\n  const cards = flashcardExamples[id];\\r\\n  const [index, setIndex] = useState(0);\\r\\n  const [back, setBack] = useState(false);\\r\\n  const faceId = useId();\\r\\n  const card = cards[index];\\r\\n  return <div className=\\"co-flash\\"><p className=\\"co-kicker\\">Recall before revealing · {index + 1} / {cards.length}</p>\\r\\n    <div className={`co-flash-face ${back ? \'co-flipped\' : \'\'}`}><strong>{card.sentence}</strong><span>Say the complete rule, then compare.</span><div id={faceId} aria-live=\\"polite\\">{back && <><strong>{card.readback}</strong><p>{card.why}</p></>}</div></div>\\r\\n    <div className=\\"co-choices\\"><button aria-expanded={back} aria-controls={faceId} onClick={() => setBack(!back)}>{back ? \'Hide answer\' : \'Reveal answer\'}</button><button disabled={index === 0} onClick={() => { setIndex(index - 1); setBack(false); }}>Previous sentence</button><button onClick={() => { setIndex((index + 1) % cards.length); setBack(false); }}>{index === cards.length - 1 ? \'Start again\' : \'Next example\'}</button></div><p className=\\"co-small\\">These examples help you recall the rule. Your attached drill gives you separate questions.</p></div>;\\r\\n}\\r\\nfunction DirectionSwitch() {\\r\\n  const [only, setOnly] = useState(false);\\r\\n  return <div className=\\"co-switch\\"><div className=\\"co-choices\\"><button aria-pressed={!only} onClick={() => setOnly(false)}>if</button><button aria-pressed={only} onClick={() => setOnly(true)}>only if</button></div><blockquote>A student enters <mark>{only ? \'only if\' : \'if\'}</mark> the student has a pass.</blockquote><Arrow left={only ? \'Enters\' : \'Has pass\'} right={only ? \'Has pass\' : \'Enters\'}/><p role=\\"status\\">{only ? \'The pass is required. Having one does not prove entry.\' : \'This rule guarantees entry for a student who has a pass.\'}</p></div>;\\r\\n}\\r\\nfunction DiagramBuilder({ id }: { id: string }) {\\r\\n  const items = diagramExamples[id];\\r\\n  const [index, setIndex] = useState(0);\\r\\n  const [left, setLeft] = useState<string | null>(null);\\r\\n  const [checked, setChecked] = useState(false);\\r\\n  const item = items[index];\\r\\n  return <div className=\\"co-lab\\"><p className=\\"co-kicker\\">Build the diagram</p><TargetSentence item={item} marked={checked && left === item.left}/><p>Both complete ideas are below. Choose the sufficient condition, the one that gives you enough to prove the other. It belongs on the left of the arrow.</p><div className=\\"co-choices\\">{orderedConditionalIdeas(item).map(value => <button key={value} aria-pressed={left === value} onClick={() => { setLeft(value); setChecked(false); }}>{value}</button>)}</div>{left && <><Arrow left={left} right={left === item.target ? item.other : item.target}/><button className=\\"co-primary\\" onClick={() => setChecked(true)}>Check my diagram</button></>}<div role=\\"status\\">{checked && <div className={`co-feedback ${left === item.left ? \'co-correct\' : \'\'}`}><strong>{left === item.left ? \'Your arrow keeps the rule.\' : \'Your arrow reverses this rule.\'}</strong>{left !== item.left && <p>{item.reversedPromise}</p>}<p>{item.why}</p><p>{item.readback}</p><button onClick={() => { setIndex((index + 1) % items.length); setLeft(null); setChecked(false); }}>{index === items.length - 1 ? \'Practice these again\' : \'Next example\'}</button></div>}</div></div>;\\r\\n}\\r\\ntype IndicatorExplanation = {\\r\\n  title: string;\\r\\n  paragraphs: string[];\\r\\n  prompt: string;\\r\\n  reasoning: string[];\\r\\n  after?: string[];\\r\\n  check?: { prompt: string; options: string[]; correct: number; feedback: string[] };\\r\\n};\\r\\n\\r\\nconst indicatorExplanations: Record<string, IndicatorExplanation[]> = {\\r\\n  \'group-1\': [\\r\\n    {\\r\\n      title: \'If: the starting condition can come last\',\\r\\n      paragraphs: [\\r\\n"}'}]
